\displaystyle\,\int_{T^{\ast}_{\cdot}M}\underline{\nabla}_{i_{1}}\dots\underline{\nabla}_{i_{L}}\xi(t,x,v){\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}|_{T^{\ast}_{x}M}}

Lemma 2.7

(Covariant derivatives of momentum functions)
.

Recalling the notation from Remark
2.5
, the following formulas hold:

(2.24a)

\displaystyle\underline{\nabla}_{j}v_{k}=\underline{\nabla}_{j}v^{0}=\underline{\nabla}_{j}\langle v\rangle_{G}=\underline{\nabla}_{j}\left(\frac{v_{k}}{v^{0}}\right)=0

(2.24b)

\displaystyle\underline{\nabla}_{j}v^{0}_{\gamma}=\left[\Gamma_{jk}^{l}-\hat{\Gamma}_{jk}^{l}\right](\gamma^{-1})^{kr}\frac{v_{r}v_{l}}{v^{0}_{\gamma}}

(2.24c)

\displaystyle\underline{\nabla}^{j+3}v^{0}=\frac{v^{\sharp j}}{v^{0}},\quad\underline{\nabla}^{j+3}\langle v\rangle_{G}=\frac{v^{\sharp j}}{\langle v\rangle_{G}},\quad\underline{\nabla}^{j+3}\left(\frac{v_{k}}{v^{0}}\right)=\frac{1}{v^{0}}\mathbb{I}^{j}_{k}-\frac{v_{k}v^{\sharp j}}{(v^{0})^{3}}

Lemma 2.8

(Frame commutators)
.

Let
\xi:P\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be sufficiently regular. Then, one has:

(2.25a)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{A}_{j}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,v^{l}{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{k}}_{lij}\mathbf{B}_{k}\xi

(2.25b)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{A}_{i},\mathbf{B}^{j}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,-\Gamma[G]^{j}_{il}\mathbf{B}^{l}\xi

(2.25c)

\displaystyle[\mathbf{A}_{i},v_{j}\mathbf{B}^{j}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,0

(2.25d)

\displaystyle[\partial_{t},\underline{\nabla}_{i}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,v_{k}\left(\partial_{t}\Gamma[G]^{k}_{ij}\right)\mathbf{B}^{j}\xi

(2.25e)

\displaystyle[\partial_{t},\underline{\nabla}^{j+3}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,0

(2.25f)

\displaystyle[\bm{X},\mathbf{A}_{i}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,\,-a^{-1}(N+1)\left[\frac{v^{\sharp j}v_{l}}{v^{0}}{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{l}}_{mji}\mathbf{B}^{m}\xi-\frac{v_{j}}{v^{0}}\Gamma[G]_{ik}^{j}\mathbf{B}^{k}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\,+a^{-1}\frac{v_{j}}{v^{0}}\nabla_{i}N\mathbf{A}_{j}\xi-a^{-1}v^{0}\left(\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}N\right)\mathbf{B}^{j}\xi

(2.25g)

\displaystyle[\bm{X},\mathbf{B}^{i}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,a^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{v^{0}}(G^{-1})^{ij}-\frac{v^{\sharp i}v^{\sharp j}}{(v^{0})^{3}}\right)(N+1)\mathbf{A}_{j}\xi

\displaystyle\,+a^{-1}\frac{v^{\sharp j}}{v^{0}}\Gamma[G]_{jl}^{i}\mathbf{B}^{l}\xi-a^{-1}\frac{v^{\sharp i}}{v^{0}}\left(\nabla_{j}N\right)\mathbf{B}^{j}\xi

Proof.

We take (
2.25a
)-(
2.25b
) from
[
AnCGS22
, (38)]
. (
2.25c
)-(
2.25e
) are immediate. (
2.25f
) and (
2.25g
) follow with (
2.25a
)-(
2.25c
) and the product rule. In particular, for (
2.25f
), one computes

\displaystyle\,v^{0}\left(\nabla_{j}N\right)\mathbf{B}^{j}\mathbf{A}_{i}\xi-\mathbf{A}_{i}\left(v^{0}\nabla_{j}N\mathbf{B}^{j}\xi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,v^{0}\left[\left(\nabla_{j}N\right)\cdot\Gamma[G]_{ik}^{j}\mathbf{B}^{k}\xi-\left(\partial_{i}\nabla_{j}N\right)\mathbf{B}^{j}\xi\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-v^{0}\left(\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}N\right)\mathbf{B}^{j}\xi\,,

∎

3.
Norm setup, local well-posedness and bootstrap assumptions

3.1.
Norms and energies

In this section, we introduce the necessary norms to state our initial data and bootstrap assumptions, as well the energies we will utilise in our bootstrap improvement mechanism.

Definition 3.1

(Norms and function spaces)
.

Let
\mathfrak{T}
be a
M_{t}
-tangent tensor, let
\xi
be a function on
P
, let
z\in M
and let
\mu\in\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+}
as well as
K\in\mathbb{N}
. We define the following semi-norms:

(3.1a)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{\dot{C}^{K}_{G}(M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\sup_{x\in M_{t}}\left(\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{K}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}\right)_{x}

(3.1b)

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{\dot{C}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\sup_{(x,v)\in T^{\ast}M_{t}}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{\mu}\left\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{K}\xi(t,x,v)\right\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}

(3.1c)

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{T}\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{G}(M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\left(\int_{M_{t}}\lvert\nabla^{K}\mathfrak{T}\rvert_{G}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.1d)

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0},0}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\left(\int_{T^{\ast}M_{t}}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2\mu}\frac{1+\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{2}}{\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{2}}\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{K}\xi(t,x,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.1e)

\displaystyle\|\xi\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0},1}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\left(\int_{T^{\ast}M_{t}}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2\mu}\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{K}\xi(t,x,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}^{2}{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.1f)

\displaystyle\|\xi(t,z,\cdot)\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0}|_{vert},0}(T^{\ast}_{x}M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\left(\int_{T^{\ast}_{x}M_{t}}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2\mu}\frac{1+\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{2}}{\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{2}}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{K}\xi(t,z,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}|_{vert}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}|_{vert}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(3.1g)

\displaystyle\|\xi(t,z,\cdot)\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0}|_{vert},1}(T^{\ast}_{x}M_{t})}:=

\displaystyle\,\left(\int_{T^{\ast}_{x}M_{t}}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2\mu}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{K}\xi(t,z,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}|_{vert}}^{2}{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}|_{vert}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

The corresponding norms when replacing
G
with
\gamma
or
\underline{G}_{(0)}
with
\underline{\gamma}_{(0)}
are defined analogously, as well as the respective
H^{K}
- and
C^{K}
-norms. The corresponding function spaces are defined by completion with the respective norm. Finally, we write
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0}}}:=\|\xi\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0},1}}
(and similar for the other Sasaki Sobolev norms) and define

(3.1h)

\|\xi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0}|_{vert},m}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}=\sup_{x\in M}\|\xi(t,x,\cdot)\|_{\dot{H}^{K}_{\mu,\underline{G}_{0}|_{vert},m}(T^{\ast}_{x}M_{t})}\,.

The following combined solution norms will be used to efficiently encode the initial data and bootstrap assumptions:

Definition 3.2

(Solution norms)
.

We define the following norms to measure the size of near-FLRW solutions:

(3.2a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(t)=

\displaystyle\,\|\Psi\|_{{H^{18}_{G}}(M_{t})}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{{H^{17}_{G}}(M_{t})}+a^{2}\|\nabla\phi\|_{{\dot{H}^{18}_{G}}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+\|\Sigma\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathcal{E}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathcal{B}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+\|G-\gamma\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{H^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+a^{-2}\|N\|_{H^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{H^{18}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}

(3.2b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{top}(t)=

\displaystyle\,a^{2}\|\Psi\|_{{\dot{H}^{19}_{G}}(M_{t})}+a^{4}\|\nabla\phi\|_{\dot{H}^{19}_{G}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+a^{2}\|\Sigma\|_{\dot{H}^{19}_{G}(M_{t})}+a^{2}\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa\|_{\dot{H}^{17}_{G}(M_{t})}+a^{2}\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{\dot{H}^{19}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\,

(3.2c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(t)=

\displaystyle\,\|\Psi\|_{{C^{16}_{G}}(M_{t})}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{15}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\Sigma\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathcal{E}\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathcal{B}\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{C^{14}_{G}(M_{t})}+a^{-2}\|N\|_{C^{14}_{G}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+\|\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-\mathfrak{p}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\jmath^{Vl}\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|S^{Vl,\parallel}\|_{C^{16}_{G}(M_{t})}

We note that we do not include the highest order lapse norm that we could control in
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
: As one can see from Section
5.1
, we can in fact control lapse energies up to order
21
, but at high orders, these scaled energy estimates become progressively weaker. Conversely, for the bootstrap assumption, we only need to assume low order lapse bounds to control nonlinear error terms, and in particular will need to use that the lapse converges to
1
near the Big Bang hypersurface. Thus, there is no benefit in including higher order lapse norms in
\mathcal{C}
for the bootstrap assumption. In fact, including the lapse in
\mathcal{H}
is redundant due to the elliptic estimates in Section
5.1
, and we only include it to for the sake of convenience.
To improve the bootstrap assumptions, we will make use of the following energies:

Definition 3.3

(Energies)
.

Let
K,L\in\mathbb{N},
and, where
K
occurs, and
0<K\leq L
. We define:

(3.3a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,(-1)^{L}\int_{M}\Psi\Delta^{L}\Psi-a^{4}\phi\Delta^{L+1}\phi\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(3.3b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,(-1)^{l}\int_{M}\langle\mathcal{E},\Delta^{L}\mathcal{E}\rangle_{G}+\langle\mathcal{B},\Delta^{L}\mathcal{B}\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(3.3c)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,(-1)^{L}\int_{M}\langle\Sigma,\Delta^{L}\Sigma\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(3.3d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,(-1)^{L}\int_{M}\left\langle\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G,\Delta^{L}\left(\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\right)\right\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(3.3e)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(N,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,(-1)^{L}\int_{M}\langle N,\Delta^{L}N\rangle_{G}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

(3.3f)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{\mu,0}(f,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{T^{\ast}M}\langle v\rangle^{2\mu}_{G}\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{L}_{vert}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert^{2}_{\underline{G}_{0}}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}

(3.3g)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{\mu,K}(f,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{T^{\ast}M}\langle v\rangle^{2\mu}_{G}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}

(3.3h)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L)}_{\mu,\leq K}(f,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\sum_{R=1}^{L}\sum_{S=0}^{\min\{K,L\}}\mathfrak{E}^{(R)}_{\mu,S}(f,\cdot)

3.2.
Initial data assumptions

Using the solution norms introduced above, we can compactly state our initial data assumption as follows:

Assumption 3.4

(Initial data assumptions)
.

Let
(M,\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{f})
be CMC initial data to the ESFV system as introduced in Section
2.3
. In particular,
\mathring{f}\geq 0
is assumed to have compact momentum support, and in the case of massless Vlasov matter, we additionally assume that
f
vanishes on an open neighbourhood of the zero section of
T^{\ast}M
.

For some sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
and some
t_{0}>0
, we assume

(3.4)

\mathcal{H}(t_{0})+\mathcal{H}_{top}(t_{0})+\mathcal{C}(t_{0})\leq\varepsilon^{2}

as well as

(3.5)

\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{11}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}})}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\,.

3.3.
Local well-posedness

For all that follows, we need to establish that solutions to the ESFV system (
1.1
) locally exist in CMC gauge with zero shift, that all solution norms and energies are sufficiently regular in time and that we can extend solutions as long as we can control the size of our variables sufficiently well. To this end, we first collect local well-posedness results in harmonic gauge from
[
Rin13
,
Sve12
]
, and then sketch how their proofs can be combined with results for the Einstein stiff-fluid system in CMC gauge to obtain the result for the ESFV system. In particular, we also collect a Cauchy stability result in harmonic gauge to argue that restricting the analysis to CMC data can be done without loss of generality (see Remark
3.7
).

Lemma 3.5

(Local well-posedness for the ESFV system in harmonic gauge)
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N},\,l\geq 3
and
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\,\mu\geq 3
. Further, let
m=0,1
denote the Vlasov mass, and let
(\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{f})
be initial data to the ESFV system (
1.1
) as in Section
2.3
with

(3.6a)

\|\mathring{g}\|_{H^{l+1}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\mathring{k}\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\mathring{\pi}\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\mathring{\psi}\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}<\infty

as well as

(3.6b)

\mathring{f}\in H^{l}_{\mu,\underline{\gamma}_{0},m}(T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}}),

Then, a local solution
(\overline{M},\overline{g},\nabla\phi,\partial_{0}\phi,f)
in harmonic gauge with Vlasov-mass
m
exists in the following sense: For some
h>0
and writing
J=(t_{0}-h,t_{0}+h)
, a spacetime manifold
\overline{M}
can be foliated by spacelike Cauchy hypersurfaces
(M_{t})_{t\in J}
, each of which is endowed with a collection of coordinates
(t\equiv x_{U}^{0},x_{U}^{1},x_{U}^{2},x_{U}^{3})
, where
x_{U}^{i}
are spatial coordinates on an open subset
U\subseteq M
and
M
is covered by finitely many such coordinate neighbourhoods. In these coordinates, the harmonic gauge constraint

\overline{g}^{\rho\sigma}\left(\Gamma[\overline{g}]^{\nu}_{\rho\sigma}-\Gamma[\overline{g}_{FLRW}]^{\nu}_{\rho\sigma}\right)=0

is satisfied, one has

(\overline{g}_{FLRW})_{ij}=a(t)^{2}\gamma_{ij}\,,

and
(\overline{M},\overline{g},\nabla\phi,\partial_{0}\phi,f)
solves (
1.1
). Furthermore, the spacetime metric components and matter variables enjoy the following regularity in these coordinates:

\displaystyle\overline{g}_{\rho\sigma}\in

\displaystyle\,C^{l-1}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l+1}(M))

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\overline{g}_{\rho\sigma}\in

\displaystyle\,C^{l-2}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l}(M))

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}\phi\in

\displaystyle\,C^{l-2}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l}(M))

\displaystyle\left((t,x)\mapsto\|f(t,x,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mu,\underline{\gamma}_{0}|_{vert},m}(T^{\ast}_{x}M)}\right)\in

\displaystyle\,C_{dt^{2}+\gamma}^{l-1}(J\times M)

For
l\geq 4
, one additionally has

(3.7)

f\in C^{l-4}_{dt^{2}+\underline{\gamma}}(J\times T^{\ast}M)\,.

If
(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]
is the past maximal interval of existence, then one has

(3.8a)

\lim_{t\downarrow\mathfrak{t}}\left(\|\overline{g}_{\rho\sigma}\|_{C^{2}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}+\|\partial_{t}\overline{g}_{\rho\sigma}\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}+\|\partial_{\mu}\phi\|_{C^{1}(M_{t})}\right)=\infty\,,

(3.8b)

\lim_{t\downarrow\mathfrak{t}}\sup_{x\in M}\left[\int_{T^{\ast}_{x}M}\langle p\rangle_{\gamma}^{2\mu}\cdot\left(\frac{\langle p\rangle_{\gamma}}{p^{0}_{\gamma}}\right)^{2l}\left(\left\lvert f(t,x,p)\right\rvert^{2}+(p^{0}_{\gamma})^{2}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}f(t,x,p)\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right)\,\mu_{\gamma}^{-1}dp\right]=\infty\,.

or
\mathfrak{t}=-\infty
.

Proof-Outline.

As argued, for example, in
[
Rin13
, Section 22.2.3]
, the Einstein equations and the wave equation for the scalar field can be modified in harmonic gauge such that one can equivalently solve a quasilinear system in
\overline{g}_{\mu\nu}
and
\phi
, coupled with the transport equation for
f
. For such systems,
[
Rin13
, Proposition 19.76 and Lemma 19.83]
lead to the stated result for
m=1
, and respectively
[
Sve12
, Paper B, Proposition 8.48 and Lemma 9.1]
for
m=0
, since one easily checks that all operators and nonlinearities remain admissible when no scalar field potential is present (see
[
Rin13
, Section 19.1]
, respectively
[
Sve12
, Paper B, Section 8.1]
). We also note that, while the results in
[
Rin13
,
Sve12
]
are proven while viewing the Vlasov distribution as a function on the mass shell, rather than the co-mass shell, we can induce initial data
\mathring{f}^{\sharp}:T^{\ast}M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
on the mass shell by

\mathring{f}^{\sharp}(x,q^{i})=\mathring{f}\left(x,(\mathring{g}^{-1})^{ij}p_{j}\right)

which, due to the regularity requirements for
\mathring{g}
, enjoys the same Sobolev regularity as
\mathring{f}
, but now with respect to the corresponding weighted Sasaki metric induced on the tangent bundle. Then working with canonical coordinates
q^{\mu}
on the mass shell, one can apply the aforementioned results and transform the corresponding solution
f^{\sharp}
back via

f(t,x,p_{i})=f^{\sharp}(t,x,\overline{g}_{i\mu}q^{\mu})\,.

Again, the regularity of the metric components that the regularity of
f^{\sharp}
transfers to
f
in the corresponding function spaces with respect to the cotangent bundle and the co-mass shell.
∎

We note that the non-negativity of the Vlasov distribution function is ensured by the Vlasov equation, and can be argued as in Lemma
4.5
. Furthermore, uniqueness follows from uniqueness of the maximal globally hyperbolic development (see Section
2.3
).

To prove that we can take initial data to be CMC without loss of generality, we need the following result:

Lemma 3.6

(Cauchy stability of near-FLRW solutions to the ESFV system)
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N},\ l\geq 3
and
\mu\in\mathbb{R},\ \mu\geq 3
, and let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small. Further, consider initial data
(M,\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{f})
to the ESFV system with Vlasov mass
m=0,1
(again as in Section
2.3
) such that one has

(3.9a)

\|\mathring{g}-\gamma\|_{H^{l+1}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\mathring{k}+\gamma\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\mathring{\pi}\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\mathring{\psi}-C\|_{H^{l}_{\gamma}(M)}<\delta

and

(3.9b)

\|(\mathring{f}-f_{FLRW})(t_{0},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{H^{l}_{\mu,\underline{\gamma}_{0},m}(T^{\ast}M)}<\delta\,.

hold. In the massless Vlasov case, we additionally assume that
\mathring{f}
vanishes in an open neighbourhood of
\{(x,p)\in T^{\ast}M\,|\,p=0\}
. Then the solution in the sense of Lemma
3.5
satisfies the following bound for some
K>0
that is independent of
\delta>0
for any
t\in[t_{1},t_{0}],t_{1}>\mathfrak{t}
:

(3.10)

\displaystyle\|g-a^{2}\gamma\|_{H_{\gamma}^{l+1}(M_{t})}+\|k-\frac{\tau}{3}a^{2}\gamma\|_{H_{\gamma}^{l}(M_{t})}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H_{\gamma}^{l}(M_{t})}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{0}\phi-Ca^{-3}\|_{H_{\gamma}^{l}(M_{t})}+\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{H^{l}_{\mu,\underline{\gamma}_{0},m}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,\leq K\delta

Proof-Outline.

For the massive case, this can be proven as in
[
Rin13
, Corollary 24.10]
, which in turn follows along similar lines as
[
Rin09a
, Theorem 15.10]
. Essentially, the argument therein reduces to using the respective result for the metric components and matter variables in harmonic gauge from
[
Rin13
, Corollary 20.7]
and patching these local results to a spatially global result. The aforementioned statements are, at first glance, only applicable for smooth initial data, but this clearly extends to non-smooth data by a standard approximation argument.

This mostly applies analogously to
m=0
– the only issue that arises is that (
3.8b
) is regularity dependent, thus solutions at high regularity might only be extendible in lower regularity. However, if the distribution function vanishes on an open neighbourhood of the zero section throughout the evolution, any choice of
l\geq 3
in (
3.8b
) is equivalent to any other (see the proof of
[
Sve12
, Paper B, Lemma 9.2]
). That this is the case can be shown as in
[
Sve12
, Paper B, Lemma 8.25]
, analogous to the proof of (
4.7
).
∎

Remark 3.7

(Existence of a CMC hypersurface)
.

Before moving on to our main well-posedness result in CMC gauge with zero shift, we illustrate why the above results imply that, as in
[
RS18b
,
Spe18
,
FU24
]
, it is is not a true restriction to take initial data to be CMC :

If the initial data is close to FLRW data as in (
3.4
), but not CMC, Lemma
3.5
yields a local-in-time solution by evolving in harmonic gauge, and by Lemma
3.6
, we can restrict ourselves to a time interval such that we remain close to the FLRW data throughout the local time evolution. An implicit function theorem argument as in
[
FK20
, Section 2.5]
now implies that, within this local solution, we can find a Cauchy hypersurface
\Sigma^{\prime}
close to the initial hypersurface
M_{t_{0}}
that is CMC. The adaptation of the argument in
[
FK20
, Section 2.5]
can be done as sketched in
[
FU24
, Remark 8.1]
: The only noteworthy change compared to the latter is in computing that
(\overline{g}_{FLRW},t_{0})
remains a regular point of the mean curvature map
H
under the addition of Vlasov matter and regardless of sectional curvature. One computes using (
2.3a
):

a(t_{0})^{2}dH_{(\overline{g}_{FLRW},t_{0})}(0,w)=\left[\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\gamma}+\kappa-4\pi C^{2}a(t_{0})^{-4}-4\pi a(t_{0})^{-2}\left(\rho_{FLRW}^{Vl}+\mathfrak{p}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\right)\right]w

Since we assume
f_{FLRW}\geq 0
and
C>0
, and since
\Delta_{\gamma}
has no positive eigenvalues, this clearly is a regular point for
\kappa\leq 0
. For
\kappa>0
,
\kappa<4\pi C^{2}a(t_{0})^{-4}
can be ensured for small enough
t_{0}>0
, which Lemma
3.6
allows us to take without loss of generality.

Having obtained a CMC hypersurface, we need to ensure that Lemma
3.6
is sufficient to ensure Assumption
3.4
for suitably small
\delta>0
. This follows from Sobolev embedding for all terms except those associated with Vlasov matter: The Vlasov equation ensures that
{f}
is still nonnegative (as in Lemma
4.5
), and that it has compact momentum support as well as, in the massless case, support bounded away from the zero section (as in Lemma
4.6
). In particular, for
\delta<1
, any momentum weights can be dropped or added up to a constant depending on
f_{FLRW}
, and thus
\|{f}-{f}_{FLRW}\|_{H^{18}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim\delta
also holds. Using a co-mass shell analogue of
[
Rin13
, Lemma 15.29]
or estimates similar to the energy bounds in Lemma
4.14
, this ensures the equivalent bound on all Vlasov matter terms in
\mathcal{C}
after potentially updating constants. Finally, (
3.5
) can be ensured since
[
Rin13
, Lemma 15.29]
applied analogously to the co-mass shell and the standard Sobolev embedding on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
imply

\|{f}-{f}_{FLRW}\|_{C^{11}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim\sup_{x\in M}\|{f}-{f}_{FLRW}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}H^{13}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}|_{vert}}(T^{\ast}_{x}M_{t})}\lesssim\|{f}-{f}_{FLRW}\|_{H^{13}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\,.

Again, we note that, on the momentum support of
{f}-{f}_{FLRW}
on
[t_{1},t_{0}]\times M
,
\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}\simeq 1
holds.

Remark 3.8

(Smallness of
t_{0}
)
.

To establish a strong total energy bound in Proposition
7.6
, we will need to assume that
a(t)
is sufficiently small for
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, which simply means that
t_{0}
needs to be sufficiently small since
a
is strictly increasing on
(0,T/2)
, see Lemma
2.2
. Lemma
3.6
ensures that, if we choose the initial perturbation to be small enough, we can use Cauchy stability to ensure that the solution remains sufficiently close to the FLRW solution in any fixed time interval
[t_{1},t_{0}]
. We can then run the argument in Remark
3.7
to find a nearby CMC hypersurface, and can thus overall assume
t_{0}>0
to be sufficiently small without loss of generality.

Having now prepared initial data to be CMC as well as sufficiently close to the Big Bang if needed, we can state our main local well-posedness result:

Lemma 3.9

(Local well-posedness in CMC gauge with zero shift)
.

Let
(M,\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{f})
be CMC initial data as in Lemma
3.5
and let
\mu\geq 3
. Additionally, assume that

\inf_{x\in M}\mathring{\psi}(x)^{2}-\lvert\mathring{\pi}\rvert_{\mathring{g}_{x}}^{2}>0\,\,.

Then, this data launches a unique solution
(\overline{M},g,k,\nabla\phi,\partial_{0}\phi,\overline{f})
of the CMC-transported ESFV-system (see (
1.1
) with CMC condition (
2.1
)) toward the past, foliated by CMC hypersurfaces
(M_{t})_{t\in J}
with
J=(t_{0}-h,t_{0}]
for some
h>0
. One has:

\displaystyle g\in

\displaystyle\,C_{dt^{2}+\gamma}^{l-1}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l+1}(M))

\displaystyle k\in

\displaystyle\,C_{dt^{2}+\gamma}^{l-2}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l}(M))

\displaystyle\nabla\phi\in

\displaystyle\,C_{dt^{2}+\gamma}^{l-2}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l}(M))

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\phi\in

\displaystyle\,C_{dt^{2}+\gamma}^{l-2}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l}(M))

\displaystyle n\in

\displaystyle\,C_{dt^{2}+\gamma}^{l}(J\times M)\cap C^{0}(J,H_{\gamma}^{l+2}(M))

\displaystyle f\in

\displaystyle\,C^{l-4}_{dt^{2}+\underline{\gamma}}(J\times TM)\cap C^{l-1}(J,L^{\infty}_{x}H_{\mu,\underline{\gamma}_{0}|_{vert},m}^{1}(T^{\ast}_{(\cdot)}M))

For the past-maximal interval of existence
(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]
, one has
\mathfrak{t}=0
or one of the following blow-up criteria are satisfied:

There exists a sequence
(t_{m},x_{m})
with
t_{m}\downarrow\mathfrak{t}
such that

(1)

such that the smallest eigenvalue of
g(t_{m},x_{m})
converges to
0
,

(2)

or such that
n(t_{m},x_{m})
converges to
0
,

(3)

or such that
\left(\lvert\partial_{0}\phi\rvert^{2}+\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert_{g}^{2}\right)(t_{m},x_{m})
converges to
0
,

(4)

or one of the following maps is unbounded:

(3.11a)

\displaystyle s\in(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]\mapsto

\displaystyle\,\|g\|_{C^{2}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}+\|k\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}+\|n\|_{C^{2}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}+\|\partial_{t}\phi\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}\,,

(3.11b)

\displaystyle s\in(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]\mapsto

\displaystyle\,\|\rho^{Vl}\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}+\|\jmath^{Vl}\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}+\|S^{Vl}\|_{C^{1}_{\gamma}(M_{s})}\,,

(3.11c)

\displaystyle s\in(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]\mapsto

\displaystyle\,\sup_{x\in M_{s}}\left[\int_{T^{\ast}_{x}M_{s}}\langle p\rangle_{\gamma}^{2\mu}\cdot\left(\frac{\langle p\rangle_{\gamma}}{p^{0}_{\gamma}}\right)^{2l}\left(\left\lvert f(s,x,p)\right\rvert^{2}+(p^{0}_{\gamma})^{2}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}f(s,x,p)\right\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right)\,\mu_{\gamma}^{-1}dp\right]

Proof-Outline.

In short, this can be proven along the same lines as in the proof of
[
RS18b
, Theorem 14.1]
for the stiff fluid, with the analysis of Vlasov matter following from
[
Rin13
,
Sve12
]
as above. The continuation criterion (
3.11a
) then extends from the scalar field case, (
3.11b
) is the direct analogue of (
3.11a
) for the Vlasov matter quantities that occur in the elliptic-hyperbolic system for the metric variables, and (
3.11c
) is needed to ensure boundedness of coefficients arising from Vlasov energy estimates as in the proof in harmonic gauge.

To be more precise, the proof of
[
RS18b
, Theorem 14.1]
relies on showing that a modified system of equations leads to an elliptic-hyperbolic system in which the second fundamental form satisfies a wave equation, where its evolution equation is considered as an additional constraint quantity. The only properties of stiff fluid matter that are used in the proof are that the stiff fluid equation implies that the associated energy-momentum tensor being divergence-free, that it satisfies the strong energy condition and that it generates standard energy estimates. The former two are also satisfied for our matter model (where we again note that one has
f\geq 0
, and thus the strong energy condition is ensured). Since scalar field matter constitutes a subcase of stiff fluid matter, the energy estimates for stiff fluids apply to the scalar field as well. Thus, energy estimates for the geometry and scalar field can be straightforwardly extended from the stiff fluid setting.

The necessary energy estimates for the Vlasov distribution function can be established as in
[
Rin13
,
Sve12
]
, where they are proven for a wide class of relativistic transport operators of the form

L\xi=\partial_{t}\xi+\frac{{z^{i}}}{q^{0}}\partial_{x^{i}}\xi+\frac{G^{i}}{q^{0}}\partial_{q^{i}}\xi\,,

where
z^{i}\equiv z^{i}[u]
and
G^{i}\equiv G^{i}[u]
are sufficiently regular coefficient functions depending on the quantities
u
that solve the coupled system of wave equations, which correspond to the spacetime metric components. Again, note that these statements are proven for the mass shell, so we need to consider the Vlasov equation on the mass shell, i.e., apply this to the equation

(3.12)

Lf^{\sharp}=\partial_{t}\xi+\frac{q^{i}}{q^{0}}\,n\,\partial_{x^{i}}f^{\sharp}+\left(-\frac{q^{i}q^{l}}{q^{0}}\,n\,\Gamma[g]^{j}_{il}-q^{0}\,g^{ij}\,\nabla_{i}n+2n\,k^{j}_{\ i}\,q^{i}\right)\partial_{q^{j}}\xi=0\,.

Nevertheless, we can establish analogous estimates for these components using the elliptic-hyperbolic system from
[
RS18b
, Theorem 14.1]
, and the transport operator for
f^{\sharp}
is actually simpler than the one that needs to be considered in harmonic gauge, energy estimates for the Vlasov distribution also extend. Finally, the components of the Vlasov energy momentum tensor can be bounded up to constant by the energies for the distribution function as in Lemma
4.14
.

Thus, we can prove local well-posedness of the modified elliptic-hyperbolic system metric and scalar field coupled to the Vlasov equation by a constructing a similar convergent sequence as in
[
Rin13
, Proposition 19.76]
and
[
Sve12
, Paper B, Proposition 8.48]
. That this then also solves the Einstein equations – i.e., that the constraints are propagated – can again be proven as in the stiff fluid setting since we only use properties of the energy momentum tensor shared by both matter models.

The blow-up criteria follow by combining what arises from the elliptic-hyperbolic system (extending from
[
RS18b
, Theorem 14.1]
) with those arising from the transport equation from Lemma
3.5
. For the latter, (
3.8a
) is covered by the requirements (
3.11a
) from the elliptic-hyperbolic system, and thus only (
3.8b
) needs to be added.
∎

Remark 3.10

(On regularity of norms and energies)
.

In addition to the initial data assumptions (see Assumption
3.4
), we can assume, without loss of generality by a standard approximation argument, that the initial data is sufficiently regular. More precisely, using Lemma
3.9
, we will assume tacitly
l\in\mathbb{N}
to be sufficiently large such that all norms and energies are continuously differentiable in time. We note that, in the massless case, the blow-up criterion (
3.8b
) is dependent on how regular we choose our initial data to be a priori. However, due to control on the support of
f
(see Lemma
4.6
), it will turn out that this additional weight can be ignored when checking this criterion (see Proof of Theorem
8.1
).

3.4.
Bootstrap assumptions

By Lemma
3.9
, initial data as in Assumption
3.4
generates a local-in-time solution toward the past such that
\mathcal{C}(t)
and
\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{11}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}
are continuous in
t
. Thus, the following bootstrap assumption can be satisfied:

Assumption 3.11

(Bootstrap assumptions)
.

Let
\sigma=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{16}}
, fix
K_{0}>0
and
c_{0}>0
such that
c_{0}\sigma<1
. We assume that, for the bootstrap time
t_{Boot}\in[0,t_{0})
, the following holds for any
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
:

(3.13a)

\mathcal{C}(t)\leq K_{0}\varepsilon a^{-c_{0}\sigma}\,.

Additionally, we assume

(3.13b)

\|\underline{\nabla}_{hor}(f-f_{FLRW})\|_{C^{10}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\leq K_{0}{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-c\sigma}

Our goal is to show that the bootstrap assumptions imply

\mathcal{C}\leq K_{1}\varepsilon a^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}},\quad\|\underline{\nabla}_{hor}(f-f_{FLRW})\|_{C^{10}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\leq K_{1}\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

for suitable constants
c_{1},K_{1}>0
and any
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, which is a strict improvement if
\varepsilon>0
is chosen sufficiently small.

Remark 3.12

(Lapse bounds)
.

We note that, in particular, (
3.13a
) implies

(3.14)

\|N\|_{C^{14}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\sigma}\,,

and we will in fact only be able to improve the convergence rate to
\varepsilon a^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
by Theorem
8.1
. Thus, the lapse converges less strongly than in the pure scalar field case, and the bootstrap assumption is also weaker (see
[
FU24
, (3.18h)]
). This is due to Vlasov matter being the asymptotically strongest term in (
2.20g
). However, it does not lead to substantial changes in the argument since the fact that the lapse converges at a rate stronger than, for example,
a(t)
for sufficiently small
\sigma>0
is enough to make otherwise borderline terms containing low orders of
N
converge.

4.
A priori estimates

4.1.
Strong low order bounds on spacetime metric and scalar field variables

In this section, we collect the necessary improved low order estimates for all variables that are not the Vlasov distribution function or associated matter quantities. Since Vlasov matter is asympotically negligible in the evolution of metric variables and the shear as well as in the Hamiltonian and Bel-Robinson constraint equations, and does not occur in the wave equation at all, all of these bounds follow almost directly from
[
FU24
, Sections 4.1 and 4.2]
, and we only sketch how one controls the occuring Vlasov terms with the bootstrap assumption.

Lemma 4.1

(Strong
C^{0}_{G}
bounds)
.

For
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, the following estimates hold:

(4.1a)

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{C^{0}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

(4.1b)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|_{C^{0}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\Sigma^{\sharp}\|_{C^{0}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

(4.1c)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}\|_{C^{0}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

Proof.

(
4.1a
)
is proven identically to the scalar field case (see
[
FU24
, (4.4a)]
), replacing the lapse bootstrap assumption therein with (
3.14
).

Regarding
(
4.1b
)
, one has

(4.2)

-\partial_{t}\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}=2{\left(\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp}\right)^{i}}_{j}{\left(\Sigma^{\sharp}\right)^{j}}_{i}\lesssim\left\lvert(\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp})^{\parallel}\right\rvert_{G}\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G}\,,

where we recall that
(\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp})^{\parallel}
is the tracefree, symmetric part of
\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp}
. Estimating all non-Vlasov terms as in the proof of
[
FU24
, (4.2b)]
using (
3.13a
), we have

\left\lvert\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp}\right\rvert_{G}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}(1+\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G})+a^{-1}\left\lvert S^{Vl,\parallel}\right\rvert_{G}\,.

That this bound is weaker than its analogue in the pure scalar field case is, again, down to (
3.14
). The Vlasov term can also be bounded by
\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}
using the bootstrap assumption (
3.13a
). Integrating (
4.2
) and inserting this bound along with (
3.4
) and (
2.6
) then implies

\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}(t)\lesssim\lvert\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}(t_{0})+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon.

The bound for
\lvert\Sigma^{\sharp}\rvert_{C^{0}_{\gamma}}
is proven identically since the bootstrap assumption on
G-\gamma
ensures that all quantities in
\mathcal{C}
satisfy the bootstrap assumptions with
C_{G}
replaced by
C_{\gamma}
, up to updating constants.

(
4.1c
)
then follows directly from (
2.20j
): Due to
\langle G,\mathcal{E}\rangle_{G}=0
, the second line in (
2.20j
) can be ignored, and using (
4.1b
) for all shear terms and (
3.13a
) for all other terms, including Vlasov matter, one has
\langle\mathcal{E},\mathcal{E}\rangle_{G}\lesssim\varepsilon\lvert\mathcal{E}\rvert_{G}
and thus the statement.
∎

Lemma 4.2

(Strong low-order
C_{G}
-bounds for spacetime and scalar field variables)
.

For
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, one has:

(4.3a)

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{C^{13}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,

(4.3b)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|_{C^{12}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,

(4.3c)

\displaystyle\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|_{C^{12}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,

(4.3d)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi\|_{C^{12}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

(4.3e)

\displaystyle\|\text{{Ric}}[G]+\frac{2}{9}G\|_{C^{10}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

(4.3f)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}\|_{C^{12}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

(4.3g)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}\|_{C^{11}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

Proof.

Regarding (
4.3b
), (
3.13a
) implies

\left\lvert\nabla^{J}\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp}\right\rvert_{G}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma},

where the Vlasov matter terms and the lapse bound (
3.14
) lead to the leading terms. Since the integral of the right hand side over
[0,t_{0}]
is still bounded by
\varepsilon
up to constant due to (
2.6
), (
4.3b
) is then proven as in
[
FU24
, Lemma 4.3]
.

(
4.3a
), (
4.3c
)-(
4.3e
) and (
4.3g
) are also proven almost entirely identically to their analogues in
[
FU24
, Lemma 4.3]
, the only differences arising again from the weaker bootstrap bound (
3.14
) on the lapse that is, as with the shear, sufficient to prove that terms containing
N
are asymptotically negligible. Finally, (
4.3g
) is proven by applying
\nabla^{J}
to (
2.20j
) and then arguing as in the
C^{0}_{G}
-case, using (
4.3b
) for the shear and (
3.13a
) for the remaining terms.
∎

Finally, we collect that the volume element
\mu_{G}
is uniformly bounded:

Lemma 4.3
.

For
\mu_{G}=\sqrt{\det G}
and
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, one has

(4.4)

\|\mu_{G}-\mu_{\gamma}\|_{C^{0}(M_{t})}\lesssim\varepsilon\,.

Proof.

This follows immediately from
\partial_{t}\mu_{G}=-N\tau\mu_{G}
, (
3.14
), the initial data assumption and the Gronwall lemma.
∎

4.2.
A priori observations for Vlasov matter

Remark 4.4

(On the charateristic method)
.

The following a priori estimates involving the Vlasov distribution function will rely on the fact that initial value problems of the form

\partial_{t}\xi+b(t,x,v)^{i}\partial_{x^{i}}\xi(t,x,v)+B_{i}(t,x,v)_{i}\partial_{v_{i}}\xi(t,x,v)+c(t,x,v,\xi)=0,\ \xi(0,x,v)=\xi_{0}(x,v)

can be locally solved using the method of characteristics, i.e.: There exist functions
X:I\rightarrow U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
V:I\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
Z:I\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with

Z(s)=\xi(s,X(s),V(s))

that satisfy the following equations:

\displaystyle\dot{X}^{i}(s)=

\displaystyle\,b^{i}(s,X(s),V(s))

\displaystyle\dot{V}_{i}(s)=

\displaystyle\,B_{i}(s,X(s),V(s))

\displaystyle\dot{Z}(s)=

\displaystyle\,-c(s,X(s),V(s),Z(s))

This is a consequence of
[
Eva98
, p. 107, Theorem 2]
. In particular, we will apply this to equations that are shifts or derivatives of the rescaled Vlasov equation (
2.19a
). Since, on the bootstrap interval, the solution to the Vlasov equation exists and is unique, it can be expressed in this fashion in the entire bootstrap interval after choosing inital data on some hypersurface
M_{t}
for
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. In particular, solutions to (
2.19a
) remain constant along integral curves
(X,V)
of the following characteristic system:

(4.5a)

\displaystyle\frac{dX^{i}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\,a^{-1}(N+1)\frac{(G^{-1})^{ij}V_{j}}{\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert V\rvert_{G}^{2}}}

(4.5b)

\displaystyle\frac{dV_{i}}{dt}=

\displaystyle\,a^{-1}(N+1)(G^{-1})^{jk}\Gamma[G]^{l}_{ik}\frac{V_{j}V_{l}}{\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert V\rvert_{G}^{2}}}-a^{-1}\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert V\rvert_{G}^{2}}\nabla_{i}N

We introduce the following notation:

\mathcal{V}=\lvert V\rvert_{G},\quad V^{0}=\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\mathcal{V}^{2}},\quad V^{0}_{\gamma}=\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert V\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}}

Lemma 4.5

(Non-negativity of the Vlasov distribution function)
.

For any
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
,
f(t,\cdot,\cdot)
is nonnegative, as well as
\rho^{Vl}(t,\cdot)
and
\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}(t,\cdot)
.

Proof.

Recall that
s\mapsto f(s,X(s),V(s))
remains constant for characteristics
(X,V)
satisfying (
4.5
) and that
\mathring{f}
is nonnegative. Thus, for any
(x,v)\in T^{\ast}M_{t}
, there exist
(x_{init},v_{init})\in T^{\ast}M
such that one has

0\leq\mathring{f}(x_{init},v_{init})=f(t_{0},x_{init},v_{init})=f(t,x,v)\,.

Since
v^{0}
is also non-negative, so are then
\rho^{Vl}(t,\cdot)
and
\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}(t,\cdot)
.
∎

Lemma 4.6

(Bounds for the momentum support)
.

There exists a constant
K>0
that is independent of
\varepsilon
such that the following bounds hold:

(4.6a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(t)\leq\mathcal{P}^{0}(t)\leq

\displaystyle\,Ka(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

(4.6b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}(t)\leq\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}^{0}(t)\leq

\displaystyle\,K

Furthermore, for
m=0
, one additionally has that the momentum support of
f
is bounded away from zero as follows:

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sup\left\{\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{-2}\ |\ x\in M_{t},\ v\in\mathrm{supp}f(t,x,\cdot)\right\}

\displaystyle\,\leq Ka(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\sup\left\{\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{-2}\ |\ x\in M_{t},\ v\in\mathrm{supp}f(t,x,\cdot)\right\}

\displaystyle\,\leq K

Proof.

Noting
\mathcal{V}\leq V^{0}
, we get the following from the characteristic equation (
4.5b
):

(4.9)

\displaystyle-\frac{d(V^{0})^{2}}{dt}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim-2\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert\partial_{t}G^{-1}\rvert_{G}\mathcal{V}^{2}+a^{-1}\lvert N+1\rvert\lvert\Gamma[G]\rvert_{G}\frac{\mathcal{V}^{3}}{V^{0}}+a^{-1}V^{0}\mathcal{V}^{2}\lvert\nabla N\rvert_{G}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\left(a^{-3}\|\Sigma\|_{C^{0}_{G}}+a^{-3}\|N\|_{C^{0}_{G}}+a^{-1}(\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{1}_{G}}+\|\gamma\|_{C^{1}_{G}})+a^{-1}\|N\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{G}}\right)(V^{0})^{2}

Using (
3.13a
) for the metric and lapse as well as (
4.1b
), this can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle-\frac{d(V^{0})^{2}}{dt}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left(\varepsilon a^{-3}+a^{-1-c\sigma}\right)(V^{0})^{2}

Updating
c>0
, the Gronwall lemma then implies

(4.10)

(V^{0}(t))^{2}\leq(V^{0}(t_{0}))^{2}\cdot a(t)^{-2c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,.

For any
x\in M_{t}
and any
v\in\mathrm{supp}f(t,x,\cdot)
,
(x,v)
must lie on a unique characteristic
(X,V)
emanating from
(X(t_{0}),V(t_{0}))\in\mathrm{supp}f(t_{0},\cdot,\cdot)
. Since
\mathring{f}
has compact momentum support, (
4.10
) implies

V^{0}(t)\lesssim\mathcal{P}(t_{0})a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

for
V(t)=v\in\mathrm{supp}f(t,x,\cdot)
, and thus (
4.6a
) after taking the supremum in
v
. (
4.6b
) is proven identically, noting that since
\partial_{t}\gamma=0
, one has

-\frac{d(V^{0}_{\gamma})^{2}}{dt}\lesssim a^{-1-c\sigma}(V^{0}_{\gamma})^{2}

and thus
V^{0}_{\gamma}
remains uniformly bounded.
(
4.7
)-(
4.8
) also follow similarly, using that
\mathring{f}
vanishes on an open neighbourhood of the zero section.

∎

To obtain strong low order bounds on the Vlasov distribution function, we will need to compare it to the reference distribution function
f_{FLRW}
while using the Sasaki connection with respect to
G
. To control the resulting error terms, we first collect the following bounds on the reference distribution:

Lemma 4.7

(Derivatives of the reference distribution function)
.

For
0<K<L\leq 19
, one has

(4.11a)

\displaystyle\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f_{FLRW}\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\,\Big(\underbrace{\sqrt{\varepsilon}\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{H^{K-1}_{G}(M_{t})}}_{\text{if }K>1}

\displaystyle\,+\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{K-1}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{H^{K-1}_{G}(M_{t})}\Big)

Additionally, for
L>0
, the following holds:

(4.11b)

\displaystyle\|\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{L}f_{FLRW}\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\,\Big(\underbrace{\sqrt{\varepsilon}\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{H^{L-2}_{G}(M_{t})}}_{\text{if }L>2}

\displaystyle\,+\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{L-1}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{H^{L-1}_{G}(M_{t})}\Big)

Finally, let
0<K<L\leq 12
. Then, one has

(4.11c)

\displaystyle\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{0}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,,

(4.11d)

\displaystyle\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-(K-1)}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K-1}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{0}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{-1-c\sigma}\,.

Recall that, by Remark
2.4
, all of the objects above are well-defined.

Proof.

Before going into the proof, we note that vertical derivatives of
f_{FLRW}
are a linear combination of terms of the form

v^{\ast_{\gamma}m_{1}}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{(m_{2})}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})

with
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
. Since we have
\mathcal{F}\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{0}^{+})
, the distribution term is uniformly bounded. Further, on the support of
f_{FLRW}
,
v
is uniformly bounded with respect to
\underline{\gamma}|_{vert}
. Thus,

(v^{0})^{L}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L}f_{FLRW}(t,x,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}}=\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{L}_{vert}f_{FLRW}(t,x,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}}\lesssim a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

holds by (
4.6a
) and (
4.3c
) for any
L>0
, as well as

\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L}f_{FLRW}\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}}\lesssim a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

(also using (
4.7
) for
m=0
).

We compute:

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{i}f_{FLRW}(\cdot,\cdot,v)=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\left[\partial_{i}(\gamma^{-1})^{jk}v_{j}v_{k}+2\Gamma_{ij}^{r}v_{k}v_{r}(\gamma^{-1})^{jk}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\left[-\hat{\Gamma}^{j}_{il}(\gamma^{-1})^{lk}v_{j}v_{k}-\hat{\Gamma}^{k}_{il}(\gamma^{-1})^{jl}v_{j}v_{k}+2\Gamma_{ij}^{r}v_{k}v_{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,2\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\,(\gamma^{-1})^{jk}\,\left[\Gamma_{ij}^{l}-\hat{\Gamma}_{ij}^{l}\right]\,v_{k}v_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,2\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\,\left[(G^{-1})^{jk}-(G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1})^{jk}\right]\,\left[\Gamma_{ij}^{l}-\hat{\Gamma}_{ij}^{l}\right]\,v_{k}v_{l}

Thus, we can estimate

\displaystyle\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{hor}f_{FLRW}(\cdot,\cdot,v)\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\lvert\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\rvert_{G}\left(1+\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{G}\right)\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{2}\chi_{\mathrm{supp}\mathcal{F}}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\,.

This proves (
4.11c
) at order
1
follows by integrating over
(T^{\ast}M,\underline{G}_{0})
and using that
\mathcal{F}
has compact support. (
4.11b
) for
L=1
follows from the same estimates by inserting (
4.3c
).

For
L=2
, notice that

(4.13)

\underline{\nabla}_{i}\underline{\nabla}_{j}f_{FLRW}=\mathbf{A}_{i}\mathbf{A}_{j}f_{FLRW}-\Gamma_{ij}^{k}\mathbf{A}_{k}f_{FLRW}-\frac{1}{2}v_{l}{{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{l}}_{kij}}\mathbf{B}^{k}f_{FLRW}

The first two terms in (
4.13
) can be dealt with as before, while for the final term, we compute

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}v_{l}{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{l}}_{kij}\mathbf{B}^{k}f_{FLRW}(\cdot,\cdot,v)=

\displaystyle\,-v_{l}{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{l}}_{kij}(\gamma^{-1})^{mk}v_{m}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left[{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{l}}_{kij}(G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1})^{mk}v_{l}v_{m}-\underbrace{v_{k}v_{l}{\text{{Riem}}[G]^{\sharp lk}}_{ij}}_{=0}\right]\cdot\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\,.

Thus, this can be estimated in
L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}
by
a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}}^{2}
using that
\mathcal{F}
is compactly supported as well as (
4.3c
) and (
4.3e
). This yields (
4.11b
) for
L=2
. For
L\geq 3
, we repeat this procedure using the product rule, incurring curvature error terms. (
4.11a
) is proven similarly, and so is (
4.11c
) using the a priori estimates (
4.3e
), (
4.3c
) and (
4.6a
).

Regarding (
4.11d
), we split up
\bm{X}f_{FLRW}
into the horizontal and vertical derivative terms and prove that both parts satisfy the claimed bound: The terms arising from the horizontal components of
\bm{X}f
can be bounded directly using (
4.11c
) since
\mathbf{A}_{i}\xi=\underline{\nabla}_{i}\xi
. For the remaining vertical components, we may encounter terms where only vertical derivatives fall on
f_{FLRW}
. However, since we are only interested in a uniform pointwise bound, we don’t need to ensure that all Christoffel terms that arise from the Sasaki connection coefficients reduce to difference tensor terms, and note that (
4.3c
) also implies the crude bound

\lvert\partial^{\leq 11}\Gamma\rvert_{G}\lesssim a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

in a coordinate neighbourhood on which
\hat{\Gamma}
remains bounded. The bound then follows for the vertical parts of
\bm{X}f
using (
3.13a
) and that
M
is compact.
∎

Lemma 4.8

(
C^{0}_{1,\cdot}
-estimates for the Vlasov distribution function)
.

For
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, one has:

(4.14a)

\displaystyle\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{0}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

(4.14b)

\displaystyle\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{0}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

Proof.

We start out by rewriting the rescaled Vlasov equation (
2.19a
) as an inhomogeneous transport equation for
f-f_{FLRW}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(f-f_{FLRW}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\bm{X}(f-f_{FLRW})-a^{-1}\frac{v^{\sharp j}}{v^{0}}(N+1)\mathbf{A}_{j}f_{FLRW}+a^{-1}v^{0}\,\nabla_{j}N\,\mathbf{B}^{j}f_{FLRW}

Let
(X,V,Z)
be the corresponding characteristics satisfying (
4.5
) emanating from
(x,v)\in T^{\ast}M_{t}
with
Z(t)=(f-f_{FLRW})(t,x,v)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{dZ}{dt}=

\displaystyle\,a^{-1}\frac{V_{l}}{V^{0}}(G^{-1})^{lj}(N+1)\mathbf{A}_{j}f_{FLRW}-a^{-1}V^{0}\,\nabla_{j}N\,\mathbf{B}^{j}f_{FLRW}

Note that, on the support of
(f-f_{FLRW})(t,x,\cdot)
,
V^{0}
can be controlled by
a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
using (
4.3c
) and (
4.6a
). We obtain in total:

(4.15)

\left\lvert\frac{dZ}{dt}\right\rvert\lesssim{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sigma}

Using the initial data assumption (
3.5
) and again (
4.6a
) and (
4.3c
) to control
v^{0}
on the support of
f-f_{FLRW}
, this implies

\displaystyle\lvert\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,x,v)\rvert\leq

\displaystyle\,\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}\lvert(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))-(f-f_{FLRW})(t_{0},X(t_{0}),V(t_{0}))\rvert

\displaystyle\,+\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}(f-f_{FLRW})(t_{0},X(t_{0}),V(t_{0}))\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}\left(\chi_{\mathrm{supp}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,x,\cdot)}(v)\right)\lvert Z(t)-Z(t_{0})\rvert+\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{0}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\left[\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}\,ds+\varepsilon^{2}\right]

(
4.14a
) now follows by (
2.6
). (
4.14b
) can be obtained by the same argument or from (
4.14a
) using (
4.3c
).
∎

Lemma 4.9

(Strong
C^{l}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}
-norm estimates for the Vlasov distribution function)
.

Let
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Then, the following holds:

(4.16)

\displaystyle\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{11}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

Note that, for
\varepsilon>0
suitably small, (
4.16
) is a bootstrap improvement for (
3.13b
).

Proof.

The following proof will require us to choose a spatial coordinate frame to express horizontal frame derivatives, so we must first establish suitable coordinate systems to cover the entire evolution: Since
M
is compact, there exists some sufficiently small
r>0
such that an open ball
B_{r}(x)
of radius
r>0
around any
x\in M
is a coordinate neighbourhood, and there exist
x_{1},\dots,x_{m}\in M
such that
\{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(x_{i})\}_{i=1,\dots,m}
covers
M
. Furthermore, for (
4.5a
),
\lvert\frac{d}{dt}X\rvert_{G}\lesssim a^{-1}
holds, which is integrable on
[0,t_{0}]
(see (
2.6
)), since
\lvert N+1\rvert\lesssim 1
holds by the bootstrap assumption (
3.14
). (
4.5a
) will describe the spatial characteristics for all systems considered in this proof. In particular, there exists a finite partition of
(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
by intervals
(t_{j},t_{j-1}]
such that
\int_{t_{j+1}}^{t_{j}}a(s)^{-1}\,ds<\frac{r}{2}
holds. Thus, for any
y\in B_{\frac{r}{2}}(x_{i})
, the characteristic
X
emanating from
y
at time
t_{j}
is contained in the coordinate neighbourhood
B_{r}(x_{i})
on
(t_{j},t_{j-1}]
. Thus, we can prove (
4.16
) for any
t\in(t_{1},t_{0}]
using local coordinates by having found a choice of coordinates that remains valid throughout the evolution, and since we can cover
M
in a finite amount of such coordinate neighbourhoods. Finally, the bounds then extend to
t_{1}
by continuity, and thus we can prove the statement iteratively on the entire bootstrap interval by proving the bound on
(t_{j},t_{j-1}]
from data emanating from
M_{t_{j-1}}
.

Having now justified that we can essentially perform this proof in local coordinates, we move on to the proof itself: First, we commute (
2.19a
) with
\mathbf{B}^{i}
to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(v^{0}_{\gamma}\mathbf{B}^{i}(f-f_{FLRW})\right)=

\displaystyle\,v_{\gamma}^{0}\mathbf{B}^{i}\partial_{t}f+\frac{\partial_{t}v_{\gamma}^{0}}{v_{\gamma}^{0}}\,v_{\gamma}^{0}\mathbf{B}^{i}(f-f_{FLRW})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\bm{X}\left(v_{\gamma}^{0}\mathbf{B}^{i}(f-f_{FLRW})\right)+m^{2}\frac{\dot{a}a}{(v_{\gamma}^{0})^{2}}\,v_{\gamma}^{0}\mathbf{B}^{i}(f-f_{FLRW})

\displaystyle\,+v_{\gamma}^{0}\mathbf{B}^{i}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}+(\mathcal{K}_{1,0})^{i}

with

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{1,0})^{i}(\cdot,\cdot,v)=

\displaystyle\,v_{\gamma}^{0}\,[\mathbf{B}^{i},\bm{X}](f-f_{FLRW})(\cdot,\cdot,v)+\frac{\bm{X}v^{0}_{\gamma}}{v^{0}_{\gamma}}\,\mathbf{B}^{i}(f-f_{FLRW})

(see (
2.25g
)). Moving to the corresponding characteristic system
(X,V,Z^{i})
,
X
and
V
again satisfy (
4.5
) emanating from
(x,v)\in T^{\ast}M_{t}
, while
Z^{i}(t)=v^{0}_{\gamma}\mathbf{B}^{i}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))
satisfies

\frac{dZ^{i}}{dt}=m^{2}\frac{\dot{a}a}{(V^{0}_{\gamma}(t))^{2}}\,Z^{i}+(\mathcal{K}_{1,0})^{i}(t,X(t),V(t))+V_{\gamma}^{0}(t)\,\mathbf{B}^{i}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}(t,X(t),V(t))\,.

The horizontal term derivative term in (
2.25g
) can be bounded with respect to
\gamma
by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-c\sigma}
using (
3.13b
) and (
3.13a
), while the remaining terms can be bounded by
a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}
using (
3.13a
). Further and using (
4.3c
) to switch metrics where needed, one computes

\lvert\bm{X}V^{0}_{\gamma}\rvert\lesssim a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\lvert\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\rvert_{G}+\lvert\nabla N\rvert_{G})V^{0}_{\gamma}\,,

so, with (
3.13a
), the remaining terms can be bounded by
\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}
. In summary, we obtain

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{K}_{1,0}(\cdot,X,V)\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-1-c\sigma}+a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}\,.

Due to (
4.11d
) and (
4.3c
) and since
f_{FLRW}
has compact momentum support with respect to
\gamma
, we also have

\displaystyle V_{\gamma}^{0}\lvert\mathbf{B}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}\,.

Thus, dropping the remaining term since it has favourable sign, one altogether has

-\frac{1}{2\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}}\frac{d}{dt}\left[\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}\right]\lesssim a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}+\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}+{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-1-c\sigma}\,.

After integrating and applying the Gronwall lemma as well as the scale factor integral bounds from Lemma
2.2
and the initial data assumption, yields

(4.17)

\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))\rvert_{\underline{\gamma}_{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\,.

Repeating the same procedure for
\mathbf{A}_{i}
, we have

\partial_{t}\mathbf{A}_{i}(f-f_{FLRW})=\bm{X}\mathbf{A}_{i}(f-f_{FLRW})+\mathbf{A}_{i}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}+(\mathcal{K}_{1,1})_{i}

with

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{1,1})_{i}=

\displaystyle\,[\mathbf{A}_{i},\bm{X}](f-f_{FLRW})+[\partial_{t},\mathbf{A}_{i}](f-f_{FLRW})\,,

where (
2.25f
) and (
2.25d
) imply, using (
3.13a
) and (
4.1b
):

\displaystyle\lvert[\bm{X},\mathbf{A}_{(\cdot)}](f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert\mathbf{A}_{(\cdot)}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\gamma}+\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\underline{\gamma}_{0}}

\displaystyle\lvert[\partial_{t},\mathbf{A}_{(\cdot)}](f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}\left(f-f_{FLRW}\right)\rvert_{\underline{\gamma}_{0}}

Using (
4.17
), it follows that

\lvert\mathcal{K}_{1,1}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert\mathbf{A}_{(\cdot)}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\gamma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{4}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

With (
4.11d
), one also computes

\lvert\mathbf{A}_{(\cdot)}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,,

and now obtains that

(4.18)

\lvert\mathbf{A}_{(\cdot)}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

holds. Finally, we can redo the proof of (
4.17
), replacing (
3.13b
) with the improved bound (
4.18
), which improves the bound in (
4.17
) to
\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
after updating constants. Altogether, one now concludes as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
that

\|\underline{\nabla}(f-f_{FLRW})\|_{{C}^{0}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}U_{t})}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

holds for suitably chosen coordinate neighbourhoods as outlined at the start of the proof, and thus the same bounds on
T^{\ast}M_{t}
. With (
4.3c
), this also implies

\displaystyle\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,.

We now sketch how to extend this procedure to higher orders inductively, since the line of argumentation is almost identical: Assume the improved bound has been proven up to
C^{{L}-1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}
for
{L}\leq 11
. Commuting (
2.19a
) with
(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{L}\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{L}}
implies the following transport system:

(\partial_{t}-\bm{X})\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i^{{L}}}(f-f_{FLRW})=(\mathcal{K}_{{L},0})^{i_{1}\dots i_{L}}+(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{L}\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{L}}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}

with

\displaystyle(\mathcal{K}_{{L},0})^{i_{1}\dots i_{L}}=

\displaystyle\,[(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{L}\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i^{{L}}},\bm{X}](f-f_{FLRW})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{L}\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{{L}-1}}[\mathbf{B}^{i_{{L}}},\bm{X}](f-f_{FLRW})+\dots+[\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}},\bm{X}]\mathbf{B}^{i_{2}}\dots\mathbf{B}_{i^{{L}}}(f-f_{FLRW})

\displaystyle\,+(\bm{X}(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{L})\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{L}}(f-f_{FLRW})

Once, again the characteristics
(X,V)
are as in (
4.5
), while one has

\frac{dZ^{i_{1}\dots i_{L}}}{dt}=(\mathcal{K}_{{L},0})^{i_{1}\dots i_{L}}+\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{L}}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}

with

(V^{0}_{\gamma})^{{L}}\lvert\mathcal{K}_{{L},0}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim a^{-1-c\sigma}(V^{0}_{\gamma})^{L}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{C_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}^{{L}-1}}

and

(V^{0}_{\gamma})^{{L}}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}^{L}\bm{X}f_{FLRW}\right\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-1-c\sigma}

This yields

\left(V^{0}_{\gamma}(t)\right)^{L}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}^{L}(f-f_{FLRW})(X(t),V(t))\right\rvert_{{\gamma}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

as with
{L}=0
.

From here, we can repeat this procedure for
(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{L-R}\mathbf{B}^{L-R}\mathbf{A}^{R}
by iterating over increasing
R
for
R=1,\dots,{L}
, where we will have proven

\left(V^{0}_{\gamma}(t)\right)^{L-\tilde{R}}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}^{L-\tilde{R}}\mathbf{A}^{\tilde{R}}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))\right\rvert_{{\gamma}}\lesssim{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{4}}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

for
0\leq\tilde{R}<R
in the previous steps. Additionally, the induction hypothesis allows us to use (
4.16
) up to order
{L}-1
. We use all of these bounds to control Vlasov terms of lower order and of order
{L}
with less horizontal derivaitves in
\mathcal{K}_{{L},R}
, along with(
4.1b
),(
4.3b
) and (
3.13a
) to control shear, metric and lapse as well as (
4.6a
) and (
4.3c
) to control superfluous momentum terms. Additionally, for
R<L
, we use the bootstrap assumption (
3.13b
) to bound the term

(4.19)

-a^{-1}(N+1)\left(\frac{1}{v^{0}}\mathbb{I}^{j}_{i_{K}}-\frac{v^{\flat}_{i_{K}}v^{j}}{(v^{0})^{3}}\right)\mathbf{B}^{i_{1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{K-1}}\mathbf{B}^{i_{K+1}}\dots\mathbf{B}^{i_{L-R}}\mathbf{A}_{i_{L-R+1}}\dots\mathbf{A}_{i_{L}}\mathbf{A}_{j}(f-f_{FLRW})\,,

which occurs when applying the product rule to the horizontal derivative term in
\bm{X}
, by
{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-1-c\sigma}
. In total and now considering
{Z^{i_{1}\dots\char 16\relax_{L-R}}}_{i_{L-R+1}\dots i_{L}}
in analogy to above, this then implies

(4.20)

\lvert\mathcal{K}_{L-R,R}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\begin{cases}a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}a^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{4}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}&R<L\\
a^{-1-c\sigma}\lvert Z\rvert_{\gamma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{4}}a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}&R=L\end{cases}\,.

We again use Lemma
4.7
to control the reference terms. In total, at order
L
, we use strong low order bounds on

\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{C_{G}^{{L}-1}(M_{t})},\ \|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{C^{{L}-1}_{G}(M_{t})},\text{ and }\|\Sigma\|_{C^{{L}}_{G}(M_{t})}\,

(along with using the bootstrap assumption (
3.14
) for
\|N\|_{C^{{L}+1}_{G}(M_{t})}
). Thus, this procedure can only be performed until
{L}=11
. Altogether, one obtains

\displaystyle\sum_{R=0}^{L}(V^{0}_{\gamma}(t))^{L-R}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}^{L-R}\mathbf{A}^{R}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))\right\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,,

and, since (
4.19
) does not occur for
R=L
, also

\left\lvert\mathbf{A}^{L}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,X(t),V(t))\right\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

just like for
L=R=1
. Now, we iterate over decreasing
R
: Assume that one has proven, for any
\tilde{R}>R\geq 0
,

\left(V^{0}_{\gamma}(t)\right)^{L-\tilde{R}}\left\lvert\mathbf{B}^{{L}-\tilde{R}}\mathbf{A}^{\tilde{R}}(f-f_{FLRW})(X(t),V(t))\right\rvert_{{\gamma}}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,.

Then, one can bound (
4.19
) by
\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
instead of applying (
3.13b
), improving the error term bound (
4.20
) as before and thus yielding the same bound as above for
\tilde{R}=R
. In total, we obtain

\sum_{r=0}^{{L}}(V^{0}_{\gamma}(t))^{{L-R}}\lvert\mathbf{B}^{{L-R}}\mathbf{A}^{R}(f-f_{FLRW})(X(t),V(t))\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,.

Finally, note that (
4.3e
) and (
4.3c
) imply

\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-R}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{R}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}\lesssim\lvert\mathbf{B}^{L-R}\mathbf{A}^{R}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lvert\underline{\nabla}^{{L}-1}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}},

and subsequently (
4.16
) at order
{L}
. This closes the induction over
{L}\leq 11
, proving (
4.16
)
∎

Additionally, the bounds on the Vlasov distribution function admit bounds on its energy-momentum tensor:

Corollary 4.10

(Strong low order bounds on Vlasov matter quantities)
.

The following bounds hold:

(4.21)

\|\rho^{Vl}-\rho^{Vl}_{FLRW}\|_{C^{11}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}_{FLRW}\|_{C^{11}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\jmath^{Vl}\|_{C^{11}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|S^{Vl,\parallel}\|_{C^{11}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

Proof.

Starting with
\rho^{Vl}-\rho^{Vl}_{FLRW}
, we compute the following:

\displaystyle\left(\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\right)(t,x)=

\displaystyle\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left[f(t,x,v)-\frac{v_{\gamma}^{0}}{v^{0}}\frac{\mu_{G}}{\mu_{\gamma}}f_{FLRW}(t,x,v)\right]v^{0}\mu_{G}^{-1}dv

Note that one has

\left\lvert v^{0}-v^{0}_{\gamma}\right\rvert=\frac{(v^{0})^{2}-(v^{0}_{\gamma})^{2}}{v^{0}+v^{0}_{\gamma}}\leq\frac{\left\lvert(G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1})^{ij}v_{i}v_{j}\right\rvert}{v^{0}+v^{0}_{\gamma}}\lesssim\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{G}\cdot v^{0}.

Furthermore, by expanding the determinant and using (
4.4
) and
\lvert\mu_{\gamma}\rvert\lesssim 1
, one obtains with (
4.3c
) that

\lvert\mu_{G}^{-1}-\mu_{\gamma}^{-1}\rvert\lesssim\lvert\det G^{-1}-\det\gamma^{-1}\rvert\lesssim a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{G}\,.

Since
f_{FLRW}
is also uniformly bounded, one now obtains:

(4.22)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\int_{\mathrm{supp}(f-f_{FLRW})}\left[\lvert(f-f_{FLRW})(\cdot,\cdot,v)\rvert+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{G}\right]v^{0}\,\mu_{G}^{-1}dv

By (
4.6a
), the integral over any polynomial of
v^{0}
is bounded by
a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
, and thus the bound follows at order
0
by applying the uniform estimates (
4.14b
) and (
4.3c
). For higher orders, note that
\nabla{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}=0
. Thus, for any of the matter quantities, we pull the covariant derivatives
\nabla^{L}
past the integral according to (
2.23
) and can directly bound the integrand uniformly using (
4.16
) and (
4.3c
), proving (
4.21
) for the energy density. The bounds for the remaining quantities are proven identically.
∎

Finally, we collect the following lemma that will be needed to bound the evolution of weights in our energy approach:

Lemma 4.11

(Evolution bound on momentum variables)
.

(4.23)

-\frac{\partial_{t}v^{0}}{v^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-3}\,,\quad-\frac{\partial_{t}\langle v\rangle_{G}}{\langle v\rangle_{G}}\lesssim\varepsilon a^{-3}

Proof.

One computes the following:

\partial_{t}v^{0}=\frac{1}{2v^{0}}\left[2m^{2}a\dot{a}+(\partial_{t}G^{-1})^{ij}v_{i}v_{j}\right]=(N+1)a^{-3}\Sigma^{\sharp ij}\frac{v_{i}v_{j}}{v^{0}}-N\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\frac{\lvert v\rvert_{G}^{2}}{v^{0}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\cdot\frac{m^{2}a^{2}}{v^{0}}

The statement then follows from (
4.1b
) and (
3.13a
), where the final summand has favourable sign and is thus dropped. The estimate for
\langle v\rangle_{G}
is obtained identically.
∎

4.3.
Semi-norm equivalences and commutators for the Vlasov distribution

In this section, we collect estimates on covariant commutator errors for functions on the mass shell, that in particular will imply that the Vlasov energies (
3.3f
)-(
3.3g
) are coercive up to error terms dependent on lower order Vlasov energies as well as metric norms and energies. This will be sufficient to deduce control of the Vlasov matter quantities in
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
from improved energy bounds in Section
7
.

Lemma 4.12

(Commutator formulas for the Sasaki metric)
.

Let
\xi
be a sufficiently regular function on
\displaystyle\cup_{t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}],x\in M}P_{(t,x)}
and let
M_{1},M_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, understanding that terms do not occur if they would involve negative orders of derivatives, the following schematic expressions hold:

(4.24a)

\displaystyle[\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{M_{2}},\underline{\nabla}_{hor}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,v\ast\text{{Ric}}[G]\ast\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}-1}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{M_{2}}\xi+v\ast\text{{Ric}}[G]\ast\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}+1}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{M_{2}-1}\xi

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{v\ast\nabla^{M_{2}}(\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G)\ast\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}}\xi}_{\text{if }M_{2}>0}+\Xi_{M_{1},M_{2},hor}\xi

(4.24b)

\displaystyle[\partial_{t},\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{M_{2}}]\xi=

\displaystyle\,v\ast\nabla^{M_{2}-2}\partial_{t}\text{{Ric}}[G]\ast\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}+1}\xi+v\ast\partial_{t}\Gamma[G]\ast\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{M_{1}+1}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{M_{2}-1}\xi

\displaystyle\,+\Xi_{M_{1},M_{2},t}\xi

If, on the momentum support of
\xi(t,\cdot,\cdot)
,
v^{0}
is uniformly bounded by
a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
up to constant, the lower order error terms can be bounded as follows:

(4.25a)

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle_{G}

\displaystyle(v^{0})^{M_{1}}\Xi_{M_{1},M_{2},hor}\xi\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\xi\|_{H_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}^{M_{1}+M_{2}-1}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{H^{M_{2}-1}_{G}(M_{t})}\|\xi\|_{C^{M_{1}+1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}

(4.25b)

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle_{G}

\displaystyle(v^{0})^{M_{1}}\Xi_{M_{1},M_{2},t}\xi\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim\varepsilon a(t)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|\xi\|_{H^{M_{1}+M_{2}-1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}

\displaystyle\,+a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|\partial_{t}\text{{Ric}}[G]\|_{H^{M_{2}-2}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\partial_{t}\Gamma[G]\|_{H^{M_{2}-2}_{G}(M_{t})}\right)\|\xi\|_{C^{M_{1}+1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}

By Lemma
4.6
, (
4.25
) applies for
\xi=f
.

Proof.

These formulas follow from straightforward computations using the commutator formulas in Lemma
2.8
and the connection coefficient expressions from (
2.17
). For the error bounds, we recall the bounds (
4.6a
) on the momentum support, and estimate lower order curvature and metric errors with (
4.3e
) and (
4.3c
) to obtain (
4.25a
). For (
4.24b
), recall (
2.20e
) and (
2.20d
) and use (
4.3b
) as well as the bootstrap assumption to bound lower order terms in the rescaled shear and lapse respectively.
∎

Lemma 4.13

(Rearrangement of derivatives in intermediate Vlasov energies)
.

Let
0<K<L
and let
\mathcal{D}_{L,K}
denote a combination of
L-K
vertical and
K
horizontal covariant derivatives with respect to the Sasaki metric
\underline{G}
. Then, the following holds:

(4.26a)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\|\mathcal{D}_{L,K}(f-f_{FLRW})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}-\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}(f-f_{FLRW})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-1)}_{1,\leq K}(f,t)+\varepsilon{\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq K-1)}(\text{{Ric}},t)}+\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{K-1}_{G}(M_{t})}^{2}+\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|_{H^{K-1}_{G}(M_{t})}^{2}\right)

Similarly, one has for
1\leq K<L
,

(4.26b)

\displaystyle\left\lvert\|\mathcal{D}_{L,K}f\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}^{2}-\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{L,K}(f,t)\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-1)}_{1,\leq K}(f,t)+\varepsilon a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq K-1)}(\text{{Ric}},t)

\displaystyle\,+a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{t})}^{2}+\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|_{H^{1}_{G}(M_{t})}^{2}\right)\,.

If one additionally takes
K\leq 10
in any of the above bounds, all terms but the Vlasov energy quantities can be dropped.

Proof.

Reviewing the commutator formulas from Lemma
2.8
, one schematically has for
K>0
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{L,K}\xi-\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}\xi=

\displaystyle\,\sum_{I=0}^{K-1}\left[v\ast\nabla^{I}\text{{Riem}}[G]\ast\underline{\nabla}^{L-I-1}\xi+\nabla^{I}\text{{Riem}}[G]\ast\underline{\nabla}^{L-I}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\,+\langle\text{lower order nonlinear terms containing }v,\text{{Riem}}[G],\xi\rangle\,

where we recall
\text{{Riem}}[G]=\text{{Ric}}[G]\ast G
. Thus, the product rule and the a priori estimates (
4.3e
), (
4.6a
) and (
4.16
) yield the following:

\displaystyle\,\left\lvert\|\mathcal{D}_{L,K}(f-f_{FLRW})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}-\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}(f-f_{FLRW})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|f-f_{FLRW}\|^{2}_{H^{L-1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}+\varepsilon a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq K-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

Furthermore, after applying the triangle inequality to
\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}(f-f_{FLRW})\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}
, we can estimate the reference terms with (
4.11a
). (
4.26a
) then follows by iterating over iteratively replacing mixed terms on the right hand side until only Vlasov energy terms remain.

For (
4.26b
), we largely proceed as above replacing
f-f_{FLRW}
with
f
. However, whenever we encounter a term with only one horizontal derivative by commuting, we replace
f
with
(f-f_{FLRW})+f_{FLRW}
. For the former, we simply use (
4.26a
). Regarding the latter, the analogous bounds hold as for
\mathcal{D}_{L,1}f_{FLRW}
as for
\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-1}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}f_{FLRW}
in (
4.11a
), except for incurring metric terms up to second order since taking vertical derivatives causes metric terms to occur at order zero that need to be differentiated: For example, one has

\underline{\nabla}_{hor}\underline{\nabla}_{vert}f_{FLRW}=\underline{\nabla}_{hor}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\ast v\ast\gamma)=v\ast\left(\underline{\nabla}_{hor}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\ast\gamma^{-1}+\mathcal{F}^{\prime}(\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2})\ast\nabla(G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1})\right)\,.

Note that, since we only need to apply (
4.11a
) when only one horizontal derivative occurs, we do not incur higher order metric errors in (
4.26b
).
∎

Since Lemma
4.13
allows us to exchange arrange covariant derivatives in arbitrary order up to curvature errors, this will imply that Vlasov energies are coercive up to lower order metric errors, along with bounds on the reference distribution function to control the purely vertical case. We will use this in Corollary
7.11
to improve bounds on Sobolev norms containing
f-f_{FLRW}
. To then improve the bootstrap assumptions, we need to show that these sufficiently control the energy momentum tensor, which is ensured by the following lemma:

Lemma 4.14

(Energy bounds for Vlasov energy-momentum tensor components)
.

For
\,L\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, the following bounds hold:

(4.27a)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\sqrt{\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{1,0}(f,t)}+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{t})}\right)

(4.27b)

\displaystyle\|\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{\dot{H}^{L}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{\dot{H}^{L}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L}(f,t)}

(4.27c)

\displaystyle\|\jmath^{Vl}\|_{\dot{H}^{L}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L}(f,t)}

(4.27d)

\displaystyle\|(S^{Vl})^{\parallel}\|_{\dot{H}^{L}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L}(f,t)}

Proof.

Note that we can apply Jensen’s inequality on continuous functions
\xi
on
P
that have compact support after renormalising, i.e., for
\lvert\mathrm{supp}\xi\rvert_{G}=\sup_{v\in\mathrm{supp}\xi(t,x,\cdot)}\lvert v\rvert_{G}
,

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v^{0}\xi\mu_{G}^{-1}dv\right)^{2}{\text{{vol}}}_{G}=

\displaystyle\int_{M}\mu_{G}^{-2}\lvert\mathrm{supp}\xi\rvert_{G}^{2}\left(\lvert\mathrm{supp}\xi\rvert_{G}^{-1}\int_{\mathrm{supp}\xi}v^{0}\xi\,dv\right)^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\int_{T^{\ast}M}\mu_{G}^{-1}\lvert\mathrm{supp}\xi\rvert_{G}(v^{0})^{2}\xi^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}\,,

and that

\mu_{G}^{-1}\lvert\mathrm{supp}(f-f_{FLRW})\rvert_{G}\lesssim(1+\mathcal{P}+\|G-\gamma\|_{C^{0}_{G}(M)})^{3}\lesssim a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}

since
\mu_{G}
is uniformly bounded due to (
4.4
) and using (
4.6a
) and (
4.3c
). Thus, recalling (
4.22
), we compute for the energy density:

\displaystyle\|\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\int_{M_{t}}\left(\int_{\mathrm{supp}(f-f_{FLRW})(t,x,\cdot)}\left[\lvert f-f_{FLRW}\rvert+\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{G}\right]v^{0}\,\mu_{G}^{-1}dv\right)^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\int_{T^{\ast}M_{t}}(v^{0})^{2}\left[\lvert f-f_{FLRW}\rvert^{2}+\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{1,0}(f,t)+a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{t})}\right)

The computations for the pressure are fully analogous, proving (
4.27a
) after updating constants. By (
2.13
) and (
2.14
), we can express
\jmath^{Vl}
and
(S^{Vl,\parallel})^{i}_{\ j}
as weighted integrals over
f-f_{FLRW}
, avoiding metric error terms. Thus, (
4.27c
) and (
4.27d
) at
L=0
follow directly by applying the Jensen inequality as above. Higher orders are proven fully analogously, observing as in the proof of Lemma
4.10
that
\nabla\rho^{Vl}_{FLRW}=\nabla\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}_{FLRW}=0
, as well as the Vlasov energy definition (
3.3g
) – this allows us directly apply the Jensen inequality to all matter variables without having to expand the integrand.
∎

5.
Estimates for spacetime and scalar field variables

All of the estimates in this and the following section hold for
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
. Furthermore, we let
\omega\gg\sigma>0
be a constant that is suitably small: For example, if
\sigma
is chosen small enough that
c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}<c\sigma<\frac{1}{1000}
holds throughout the proof,
\omega=\frac{1}{100}
is an admissible choice, but this choice is far from optimal. In general,
\omega
only needs to be small compared to the order of derivatives used in our estimates, but large compared to
\sigma
.

5.1.
Lapse energy estimates

In this section, we collect estimates for the lapse that follow from (
2.20f
)-(
2.20g
). In particular, in contrast to the estimates in
[
FU24
, Section 5]
, we will also prove estimates for odd order lapse energies, since these will be necessary to control high order lapse terms.

Definition 5.1

(Elliptic operators)
.

(5.1a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\zeta=

\displaystyle\,a^{4}\Delta\zeta-h\cdot\zeta,

\displaystyle h=

\displaystyle-3\kappa a^{4}+12\pi C^{2}+12\pi a^{2}\left({\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}+{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\right)+H

\displaystyle H=

\displaystyle\,\langle\Sigma,\Sigma\rangle_{G}+8\pi\Psi^{2}+16\pi C\Psi+4\pi a^{2}\left(\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}+3\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-3{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\right)

(5.1b)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\zeta=

\displaystyle\,a^{4}\Delta\zeta-\tilde{h}\cdot\zeta,

\displaystyle\quad\tilde{h}=

\displaystyle-3\kappa a^{4}+12\pi C^{2}+12\pi a^{2}({\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}+{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl})+\tilde{H}

\displaystyle\tilde{H}=

\displaystyle\,a^{4}\left[R[G]+\frac{2}{3}-8\pi\lvert\nabla\phi\rvert^{2}_{G}\right]+12\pi a^{2}\left[(\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl})-(\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl})\right]

With these operators, the rescaled lapse equations (
2.20f
)-(
2.20g
) then read

(5.2)

\mathcal{L}N=\,H\text{ and }\tilde{\mathcal{L}}N=\,\tilde{H}\,,

and admit the following estimates:

Lemma 5.2

(Elliptic estimates for
\mathcal{L}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}
)
.

For scalar functions
\zeta,Z
on
M_{t}
with
\mathcal{L}\zeta=Z
or
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}\zeta=Z
, one has

(5.3)

a^{4}\|\Delta\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M)}+a^{2}\|\nabla\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M)}+\|\zeta\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M)}\lesssim\|Z\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M)}

Proof.

The only difference in the proof in the pure scalar-field case is that Vlasov terms occur in
h
and
\tilde{h}
. However, we observe that
{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}+{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}
is nonnegative and only depends on
t
, while one has
\lvert H\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon
by the (
4.1b
), (
4.1a
) and (
3.13a
), as well as
\lvert\tilde{H}\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon a^{2-c\sigma}
by (
3.13a
