\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L}(f,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L-1}(f,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L)}(\Sigma,s)+\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L)}(\phi,s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot\left(a(s)^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},s)+\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\right)\,ds

For
L\leq 10
, the curvature energies can be dropped. For
L=1
, one has

(6.17)

\displaystyle a(t)^{4+2\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{2}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)\cdot a(s)^{4+2\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{2}}\cdot a(s)^{4+\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,0}(f,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{5}{4}\omega}\cdot a(s)^{4}\left(a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,s)+\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}(\phi,s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{3}{2}\omega}\,\cdot a(s)^{4+\frac{\omega}{2}}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{3}{2}\omega}\,\cdot a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\,ds

(6.18)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,0}(f,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 1)}(\Sigma,s)+\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 1)}(\phi,s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,\left(\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\right)\,ds

Proof.

The proof of these estimates is analogous to that of Lemma
6.6
since (
6.4a
) is just a simpler version of (
6.3a
), and the commutator formula (
4.24b
) is also used for
M_{1}=0
in a similar fashion to the previous proof. As a difference in estimation, we note that the high order curvature term present in (
6.4a
) is now of order
L-1
, and thus needs to be thought of as being of order
L+1
in the metric, i.e., of higher order than the Vlasov energy itself. Consequently, the resulting energy controlling this term needs to be additionally scaled to allow the argument to close, which makes the resulting energy term borderline.
∎

7.
Improving the Bootstrap assumptions

In this section, we combine the energy estimates from Sections
5
, and
6
to improve the bootstrap assumption (
3.13a
) – recall that (
3.13b
) is already improved due to (
4.16
). To this end, we first derive improved energy bounds for the spacetime and scalar field variables and then use these to derive bounds for Vlasov energies. We then translate these bounds to Sobolev norm bounds, and finally to bounds on
\mathcal{C}
.

In the following, we again assume
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
and that
\omega\gg\sigma
is a suitably small constant, see also the start of Section 5.

To deal with linear borderline terms in the energy estimates for the shear (see Lemma
5.9
) and Bel-Robinson variables (see Lemma
5.8
), we need to extend
[
FU24
, Lemma 7.1]
to ensure that the error terms essentially have favourable sign even for flat and spherical spatial geometry:

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
L\in 2\mathbb{N}
. Then, the following estimate is satisfied:

(7.1)

\displaystyle\,\int_{M}\left[16\pi C^{2}a^{-3}(N+1)\langle\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\mathcal{E},\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rangle_{G}+8\pi C^{2}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}^{2}+6\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(N+1)\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\mathcal{E}\rvert_{G}^{2}\right]{\text{{vol}}}_{G}

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\geq 0&\kappa\leq 0\\
\gtrsim-a^{-1}\left[4\pi C^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)\right]&\kappa>0\end{cases}

Proof.

Firstly, the bootstrap assumption (
3.14
) implies
N+1>0
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough. For
\kappa\leq 0
, we have
a^{-3}\leq\frac{3}{4\pi C^{2}}\frac{\dot{a}}{a}
by (
2.3a
) since the Vlasov density is nonnegative. Then, the statement follows directly from this and the Young inequality as in
[
FU24
, Lemma 7.1]
. Else, one has

a^{-3}\leq\frac{3}{4\pi C^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{\dot{a}^{2}+\kappa}}{a}\leq\frac{3}{4\pi C^{2}}\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}+\sqrt{\kappa}a^{-1}\right)

and thus the integrand on the left hand side in (
7.1
) is bounded from below by

\gtrsim-a^{-1}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\Sigma\rvert_{G}\lvert\Delta^{\frac{L}{2}}\mathcal{E}\rvert_{G}\,,

implying the statement.
∎

To obtain bounds on the spacetime and scalar field energies, we need to combine Vlasov energies at order
L
with appropriate scaling as follows:

Definition 7.2

(Total scaled Vlasov energies)
.

For
0\leq L\leq 19
, we define

(7.2)

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}=\sum_{K=0}^{L}a^{(K+1)\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)\,.

With this in hand, we can combine the estimates from Section
6
as follows:

Lemma 7.3

(Total scaled Vlasov energy estimate)
.

For
L=1
, one has

(7.3)

\displaystyle a(t)^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{total,Vl}

\displaystyle(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-{\omega}-c\sigma}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)a(s)^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{total,Vl}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{2}}\cdot a(s)^{4}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 1)}(\phi,s)+a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 1)}(\Sigma,s)+\varepsilon^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\left(a(s)^{4+\frac{\omega}{2}}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}+a(s)^{4+\frac{\omega}{2}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\right)\,ds

Let
L\geq 2
. Then, the following holds:

(7.4)

\displaystyle a(t)^{\beta}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-{\omega}-c\sigma}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)a(s)^{\beta}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{4}}\cdot a(s)^{\beta}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\phi,s)+a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+{(L-1)}\omega}\cdot a(s)^{4+\beta}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+L\omega}\cdot a(s)^{\beta}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{4}}\cdot a(s)^{\beta}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\phi,s)+a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}(\Sigma,s)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-\omega-c\sigma}+a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{2}}\right)a(s)^{\beta+(L-1)\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}_{1,\leq L-2}(f,s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\omega}+\varepsilon a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\omega}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-4)}(\text{{Ric}},s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\Big(a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\beta}+a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\beta+\omega}\Big)\cdot

\displaystyle\,\qquad\cdot\left(a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{1}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}+a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{H^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\right)\,ds

Proof.

This is a combination of Lemma
6.5
,
6.6
and
6.7
. Observe that the final line arises from Lemma
6.5
, while the remaining lines arise from Lemma
6.6
for
K=L-1
and Lemma
6.7
. Furthermore, we immediately rewrote
\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}_{1,\leq K}(f,\cdot)
as follows: First, let
\mathcal{K}_{L,K}>0
be a suitable constant depending on
L
and
K
and
\underline{\nabla}_{v}=v_{l}\underline{\nabla}_{\mathbf{B}^{l}}
. For
L-1>K>0
, we integrate by parts:

\displaystyle a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)=

\displaystyle\,-a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\int_{T^{\ast}M}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2}(v^{0})^{2(L-K-1)}\langle\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K-2}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f,\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f\rangle_{\underline{G}}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}

\displaystyle\,-2a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\int_{T^{\ast}M}\left((L-K-1)(v^{0})^{2(L-K-2)}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2}+(v^{0})^{2(L-K-1)}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\,\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\langle\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K-2}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f,\underline{\nabla}_{v}\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K-2}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f\rangle\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}

Integrating by parts again for the second term, one obtains

\displaystyle a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)+a^{-2c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-2)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)\right)

\displaystyle\,+\mathcal{K}_{L,K}a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\int_{T^{\ast}M}(v^{0})^{2(L-K-2)}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L-K-2}\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{K}f\rvert_{\underline{G}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-2)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)\,.

For
K=L-1>1
, one can argue identically without an error term after the first integration by parts since
\underline{\nabla}_{hor}v^{0}=0
, obtaining

a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}_{1,L-1}(f,\cdot)\lesssim\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L}(f,\cdot)+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-2)}_{1,L-2}(f,\cdot).

For
K=0
, one can argue as above replacing
f
with
f-f_{FLRW}
. Finally, for
L-1=1=K
, we need to perform the following replacement after integrating by parts:

\displaystyle\,a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{5}{2}\omega+\beta}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,\cdot)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{3}{2}\omega+\beta}\left[-\int_{T^{\ast}M}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2}(f-f_{FLRW})\cdot({G}^{-1})^{ij}\underline{\nabla}_{i}\underline{\nabla}_{j}f\,{\text{{vol}}}_{\underline{G}}\right.

\displaystyle\,-\left.\int_{T^{\ast}M}\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2}(f-f_{FLRW})({\underline{G}}^{-1})^{ij}\underline{\nabla}_{i}\underline{\nabla}_{j}f_{FLRW}+\langle v\rangle_{G}^{2}\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{hor}f_{FLRW}\rvert_{\underline{G}_{0}}^{2}\,{\text{{vol}}}_{G}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-3+\frac{\omega}{2}}\left(a^{\beta+3\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(2)}_{1,2}(f,\cdot)+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a^{\beta+\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{1,0}(f,\cdot)\right)

\displaystyle\,+a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\beta+\frac{5}{2}\omega}\|\underline{\nabla}_{hor}f_{FLRW}\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}}(T^{\ast}M)}^{2}+a^{3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\beta+\frac{7}{2}\omega}\|\underline{\nabla}_{hor}^{2}f_{FLRW}\|_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}}(T^{\ast}M)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-3+\frac{\omega}{2}}\left(a^{\beta+3\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(2)}_{1,2}(f,\cdot)+a^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\cdot a^{\beta+\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{1,0}(f,\cdot)\right)

\displaystyle\,+a^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\beta+2\omega}\left(a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{1}_{G}(M)}^{2}+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{G}(M)}\right)\,

The final step follows by applying (
4.11b
).
∎

Recalling the discussion in Section
1.4.2
, we would like to construct a total energy as in
[
FU24
]
that admits a strong bound via the Gronwall lemma. To this end, we essentially take the energy
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}
that describes the quiescent formalism as in
[
FU24
]
and add the total Vlasov energy to it, which forces us to add further scaled terms to the total energy
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}
. Before explaining the precise rationale behind these terms, we introduce these energies.

Definition 7.4

(Total energies)
.

We define:

(7.5a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total}=

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}+a^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\right)\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)

\displaystyle\,+a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}(\phi,\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\right)\,a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total,Vl}+a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{total,Vl}+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M)}^{2}+a^{4+\frac{\omega}{2}}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{G}(M)}

(7.5b)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}=

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)+a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)

and, for
L\in 2\mathbb{N},2\leq L\leq 18

(7.5c)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}=

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}+a^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\right)\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\right)a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}+a^{\omega}\right)a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}+a^{\omega}\right)a^{8}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}+a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L+1)}_{total,Vl}

\displaystyle\,+\underbrace{a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\left(\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{1}_{G}(M)}^{2}+\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|_{H^{2}_{G}(M)}^{2}\right)}_{\text{if }L=2}

(7.5d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}=

\displaystyle\,\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(W,\cdot)+4\pi C^{2}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}(\Sigma,\cdot)\right)+a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L+1)}(\phi,\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L+1)}(\Sigma,\cdot)

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-2)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)+\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{4}}a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L-1)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)

Remark 7.5

(On the construction of
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}
)
.

Essentially, the total energy can be split into three components –
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}
which describes the core quiescent mechanism as in
[
FU24
]
,
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}
which is a natural energy for Vlasov matter in near-FLRW regimes, and various scaled energies controlling components of the the spacetime metric. We briefly explain why the latter needs to be introduced when one attempts to combine
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}
and
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}
into a total energy that admits a useful Gronwall estimate:

First, we notice that Vlasov energies occur linearly in energy estimates for the scalar field, see Lemma
5.5
, and also vice versa, see (
7.4
). Thus, we do not gain smallness in terms of
\varepsilon
, and the Vlasov energy must enter the total energy without
\varepsilon
-weights. Second, Vlasov matter also linearly couples linearly in the geometric evolution equations for the shear and for Bel-Robinson variables, see Lemmas
5.12
and
5.8
, and vice versa, see (
7.4
). However, to obtain a closed energy estimates for the quiescent components, one needs to introduce an
\varepsilon
-weight in
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}
as in
[
FU24
]
. On the other hand, geometric energies occur linearly in (
7.4
). Thus, to obtain closed estimates, we must introduce geometric energies that are not weighted by
\varepsilon
. To nevertheless surpress linear scalar field terms of order
t^{-1}
in the geometric estimates, we must then introduce scale factor weights on these terms, which must be weaker than those on Vlasov matter. This then extends to Ricci curvature terms as well as low order geometric error terms in the Vlasov equation, which must both exhibit stronger scaling than the second fundamental form, which occurs linearly at order
t^{-1}
in the evolution equations of either. For the second fundamental form at order
L+1
, the additional scale factor weight must be stronger than the one at order
L
so that, after applying Lemma
5.10
, all resulting terms can be absorbed into the left hand side if
a(t_{0})
is sufficiently small. The curvature term at order
L_{1}
must, in turn, be scaled more strongly than the second fundamental form at order
L+1
as before, and the top order curvature term must be similarly strongly weighted so that one can absorb terms resulting from (
5.17g
) into the left hand side.

Proposition 7.6

(Total energy bound)
.

For
L\in 2\mathbb{N},L\leq 18
, the following holds:

(7.6)

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

We start proving the case of
L=0
by combining the following energy estimates:

•

For the first line, we use (
5.7a
) for
L=0
for the scalar field energy. For the Bel-Robinson and shear energies, we treat their sum scaled by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
and their sum scaled by
a^{\frac{\omega}{4}}
separately. For the former, we apply (
5.11b
) and (
5.12b
) for
L=0
, scaled by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
. For the latter, we apply (
5.11a
) and (
5.12a
) for
L=0
.

•

For the second line, we apply (
5.7b
) for
L=1
and the elliptic shear estimate (
5.14b
) scaled by
\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\right)

•

For the third line, we apply (
6.5a
), (
7.3
) and the metric estimate (
5.15a
).

Notice that, by (
7.1
), the indefinite terms on the left hand sides of the analogues of (
5.11b
) and (
5.12b
), respectively (
5.11a
) and (
5.12a
), are nonnegative for
\kappa\leq 0
, and else are so up to a term that can be pulled to right and estimated from above by
\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total}(s)\,ds
. This leads to the following estimate:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{8}}+a(s)^{-2-c\sigma-2\omega}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total}(s)\,ds+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}+a(t)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total}(t)

Recall that we can choose both
\varepsilon>0
and
a(t_{0})
to be sufficiently small without loss of generality, see Remark
3.8
for the latter. Thus, the final term can be absorbed into the left hand side after adapting implicit constants, and the Gronwall lemma then yields

\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

For
L\geq 2
, we proceed iteratively, assuming the bound to have been shown up to
{\mathfrak{E}}_{total}^{(\leq L-2)}
. One now similarly combines the following estimates, going through (
7.5c
).

•

For the first line: (
5.7a
) for the scalar field energy, (
5.11b
), (
5.12b
) and (
5.17c
) for the Bel-Robinson, shear and Ricci energies scaled by
\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
, and (
5.11a
), (
5.12a
) and (
5.17a
) for the energies scaled by powers of
a^{\frac{\omega}{4}}

•

For the first two terms in the second line: (
5.7b
) and (
5.14a
)

•

For the curvature energies second line: (
5.17c
) and (
5.17a
), respectively (
5.17f
) and (
5.17e
) for
L=2
, as well as (
5.17d
) and (
5.17b
) for the energies at order
L-1

•

For the curvature energy in the third line: (
5.17g
) with
l=L
, scaled appropriately

•

For the Vlasov energies in the second line: (
7.4
) with
\beta=0
at order
L
and with
\beta=4
at order
L+1

•

For the metric terms if
L=2
: (
5.16a
) with
l=2,\beta=0
and (
5.15a
) with
l=2

Note that, for
L=2
, the metric and Christoffel symbol norms become a part of the total energy, and else they are absorbed into the lower order terms since they have already been improved.

Altogether, this yields the following:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left[\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{8}}+a(s)^{-2-c{\sigma}-(L+2)\omega}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}(s)\right.

\displaystyle\,\qquad\qquad\left.+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{8}}+a(s)^{-2-c{\sigma}-(L+1)\omega}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}_{total}(s)\right]\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}+a(t)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}(t)+\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}+a(t)^{\frac{\omega}{4}}\right)a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}_{total}(t)

Since the statement has already been shown for
\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}_{total}
, this implies after rearranging the first term in the final line as before that

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{\omega}{8}}+a(s)^{-2-c\sigma-(L+2)\omega}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}(s)\,ds

and thus

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

∎

Proposition 7.7

(Quiescent energy bound)
.

For
L\in 2\mathbb{N},L\leq 18
, the following holds:

(7.7)

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

In short, the strategy in this step of the argument is to rerun the argument that yielded (
7.6
), but using this bound to directly estimate all Vlasov terms and metric errors, since it directly implies, for any
l\in\mathbb{N},l\leq 18
and any
m\in\mathbb{N},m\leq l
,

(7.8)

a^{(m+1)\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(l)}_{1,m}(f,\cdot)+a^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\left(\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{G}}+\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{1}_{G}}^{2}\right)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

For
L=0
, we use the analogues of (
5.7a
), (
5.11b
), (
5.12b
) and (
5.7b
) for
L=0
and the elliptic shear estimate (
5.14b
). This yields the following:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}}+a(s)^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\,\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}(s)

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}-2\omega}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total,Vl}(s)+a(s)^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{total,Vl}+a(s)^{\frac{\omega}{2}}\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}(t)+a(t)^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}-\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{total,Vl}(t)

After pulling the first term in the last line to the left and applying (
7.6
), this implies

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}}+a(s)^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\,\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}(s)

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{4}a(s)^{-2-2\omega-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,ds+\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{4-\omega-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Since Lemma
2.2
implies that the second line can be bounded by
\varepsilon^{4}
, the Gronwall lemma leads to
\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{quiesc}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
.

We now proceed iteratively for
L\in 2\mathbb{N},L\leq 18
, assuming that one has shown

\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}_{quiesc}\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

We combine the following estimates to control the individual terms in (
7.5d
).

•

For the first line: (
5.7a
), (
5.11b
), (
5.12b
), (
5.7b
), (
5.14a
)

•

For the curvature energies second line: (
5.17c
) (respectively (
5.17f
) for
L=2
) and (
5.17d
)

This yields the following as before.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}(t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}}+a(s)^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}-(L+2)\omega}\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L)}_{total,Vl}(s)+a(s)^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(L+1)}_{total,Vl}(s)+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{G}(M_{s})}^{2}\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-2)}_{quiesc}(s)\,ds

\displaystyle\,+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}(t)+a(t)^{2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}-(L+1)\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{total,Vl}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}+a(s)^{-3+\frac{\omega}{4}}+a(s)^{-2-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}(s)\,ds+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{quiesc}(t)

The bound (
7.7
) now follows at order
L
after rearranging and applying the Gronwall lemma, proving the statement.
∎

Corollary 7.8

(Sobolev norm estimates for the solution variables)
.

(7.9a)

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{2}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.9b)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.9c)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}+\|\mathcal{B}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.9d)

\displaystyle\|\text{{Ric}}[G]-2\kappa G\|_{H^{16}_{G}(M)}+\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{17}_{G}(M)}+\|G^{\pm 1}-\gamma^{\pm 1}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.9e)

\displaystyle\|N\|_{H^{16}_{G}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Further, one has the following high order bounds:

(7.10a)

\displaystyle a^{4}\mathfrak{E}^{(17)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(7.10b)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{H^{17}_{G}}+a^{4}\|\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\|_{\dot{H}^{18}_{G}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

The first three bounds as well as the Ricci bounds follow directly from Proposition
7.6
using
[
FU24
, Corollary 4.5]
to switch bound the respective Sobolev norms up to curvature energies. To obtain the lapse bounds, we apply (
5.6b
) and (
5.6c
), where we observe that, for suitable
\omega
, we can estimate all Vlasov energies using (
7.8
). The metric and Christoffel bounds then follow from (
5.15b
) and (
5.16b
).
∎

With these improvements in hand, we are now able to derive energy bounds for Vlasov matter that are not scaled:

Proposition 7.9

(Vlasov energy bound)
.

The following bound holds:

(7.11)

\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 18)}_{1,\leq 18}(f,\cdot)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
7.6
, we again iterate over the number of derivatives, this time in increments of
1
starting from
L=1
after treating
L=0
as a special case first:

For
L=0
, note applying the bounds from Corollary
7.8
to the energy estimate (
6.5b
) implies

\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{1,0}(f,t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(0)}_{1,0}(f,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{29}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a(s)^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,ds\,.

The stated bound then follows with the Gronwall lemma.

For
L=1
, we start with (
6.9
). Notice that, using (
7.8
), the term containing the Vlasov energy of higher horizontal order
\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,\cdot)
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-2\omega}\cdot a(s)^{2\omega}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,s)\,ds\lesssim\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1-2\omega}\cdot\varepsilon^{4}a(s)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,ds\,.

Using the bounds from Corollary
7.8
on the remaining energies similarly, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,0}(f,t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 1)}_{1,0}(f,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a(s)^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{29}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,ds\,

and thus, again applying the Gronwall lemma,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,0}(f,t)\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

To deduce the bound on
\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,\cdot)
, we consider (
6.18
) and dedude that the respective integral containing
\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,0}(f,t)
therein can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\varepsilon^{\frac{15}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,0}(f,s)\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{21}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Applying the bounds from Corollary
7.8
for the remaining energies proves the bound for
\mathfrak{E}^{(1)}_{1,1}(f,\cdot)
with the Gronwall lemma as before.

For
1<L\leq 18
, assume the statement has been shown up to
L-1
. Starting again with the vertical energy estimate (
6.8
) for
\beta=0
, the bound

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,0}(f,\cdot)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

follows by the same argument as for
L=1
, in particular using (
7.8
) to bound
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,1}(f,\cdot)
. Next, let
K\in\mathbb{N},L>K>0
and assume the bound to hold for
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,\leq K-1}(f,\cdot)
, and consider the intermediate energy estimate (
6.12
). Again, notice that

\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-1}\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K+1}(f,s)\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

holds by (
7.8
), and on the other hand,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+a(s)^{-1-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\right)\left(\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K-1}(f,s)+\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq L-1)}_{1,\leq K}(f,s)\right)\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

is satisfied since improved bounds have been established for all energies in the integrand. The shear and scalar field energies can be treated as before, and (
7.9
) leads to similar terms for curvature and metric error terms that are not at top order. At highest order (i.e.,
L=18
), we note that high order curvature terms that occur in (
7.8
) are appropriately scaled by (
7.10
). Altogether, (
6.12
) then leads to the following estimate:

\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K}(f,\cdot)\lesssim\varepsilon^{4}+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}\left(a(s)^{-1-c\sigma}+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}a(s)^{-3}\right)\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,K}(f,s)+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a(s)^{-1-\omega-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon^{\frac{29}{8}}a(s)^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,ds,

and thus
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,\leq K}(f,\cdot)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
. Having now proven the bound up to
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,\leq L-1}(f,\cdot)
by iterating over
K
, we can apply the same argument to (
6.16
). Again, note that even at highest order, the metric and curvature bounds (
7.9
) and (
7.10
) are sufficient. This implies the stated bound for
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,\leq L}(f,\cdot)
, completing the iteration step and thus proving (
7.11
).
∎

Now, we are in the position to improve the remaining bootstrap assumption (
3.13a
):

Corollary 7.10

(Bootstrap improvement)
.

For
\mathcal{C}
as in (
3.2c
) and
t\in(t_{Boot},t_{0}]
, the following holds:

(7.12)

\mathcal{C}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

Regarding the Vlasov matter quantities, note that Lemma
4.14
only requires bounds on
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,L}(f,\cdot)
for
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Inserting (
7.11
) and the metric bound from (
7.9
) then implies

\displaystyle\|\rho^{Vl}-{\rho}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}+\|\mathfrak{p}^{Vl}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{FLRW}^{Vl}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}+\|\jmath^{Vl}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}+\|(S^{Vl})^{\parallel}\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Furthermore, applying the above along with (
7.9a
) and (
7.9b
) to Lemma
5.6
for
0\leq l\leq 18
yields

\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{18}_{G}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

after updating
c>0
. This, along with Corollary
7.8
, proves
From here, the statement follows for all variables measured by
\mathcal{C}
from the Sobolev embedding
H^{l+2}_{\gamma}(M)\hookrightarrow C^{l}_{\gamma}(M)
for
l\in\mathbb{N}
, as well as the estimates in
[
FU24
, Lemma 7.4]
to relate norms with respect to
\gamma
and
G
to one another.
∎

Additionally, Proposition
7.9
implies the following bound on the distribution function compared to the reference data:

Corollary 7.11

(Sobolev norm bound for the Vlasov distribution function)
.

On the bootstrap interval, one has the following:

(7.13)

\displaystyle\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{H^{18}_{1,{\underline{G}_{0}}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

For
L\leq 18
, we sum (
4.26b
) over
K=1,\dots,L-1
as well as all possible derivative combinations represented by
\mathcal{D}_{L,K}
.
Note that the curvature error terms in (
4.26b
) only necessitate control of the curvature energy
\mathfrak{E}^{(\leq 16)}(\text{{Ric}},\cdot)
as well as low order norms for the Christoffel symbols and the metric, all of which are covered in Corollary
7.8
. Thus, inserting the Vlasov energy bound (
7.11
) and Corollary
7.8
then implies

\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{L}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}-\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L}f\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Furthermore, by Lemma
4.7
and Corollary
7.8
, one also has

\|f_{FLRW}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{L}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}-\|\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{L}f_{FLRW}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

The statement then follows by expanding
\|f-f_{FLRW}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{L}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}
and using (
7.8
) to bound
\mathfrak{E}^{(L)}_{1,0}(f,\cdot)
.
∎

8.
The main theorem

We are now in the position to prove the main theorem of this paper:

Theorem 8.1

(Past stability of FLRW solutions to the ESFV system)
.

Consider FLRW solutions of the form (
1.2
) to the Einstein scalar-field Vlasov equations (
1.1
) for which, in the massless case,
f_{FLRW}
vanishes on an open neighbourhood of the zero section of
P
. Let
(M,\mathring{g},\mathring{k},\mathring{\pi},\mathring{\psi},\mathring{f})
be CMC initial data to the Einstein scalar-field Vlasov system (see Subsection
2.3
) that is
\varepsilon^{2}
-close to the FLRW data on
M_{t_{0}}
in the sense of Assumption
3.4
.

Then, the past maximal globally hyperbolic development
(\overline{M},\overline{g},\phi,\overline{f})
to the Einstein scalar-field Vlasov system (
1.1
) admits a foliation by CMC Cauchy hypersurfaces
M_{s}=t^{-1}(\{s\}),\ s\in(0,t_{0}]
such that the solution norms
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
(see Definition
3.2
) satisfy

(8.1a)

\mathcal{H}(t)+\mathcal{C}(t)\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

and one has

(8.1b)

\|(f-f_{FLRW})\|_{C^{11}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{-c\sqrt{\varepsilon}}\,.

In particular, this implies the following asymptotic behaviours for the solution variables: There exist a scalar function
\Psi_{Bang}\in C_{\gamma}^{14}(M)
, a
(1,1)
-tensor field
K_{Bang}\in C^{14}_{\gamma}(M)
, a volume form
{\text{{vol}}}_{Bang}\in C^{14}_{\gamma}(M)
and a
(0,2)
-tensor field
M_{Bang}\in C^{14}_{\gamma}(M)
such that the following bounds hold for any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
:

(8.2a)

\displaystyle\|n-1\|_{C^{14}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.2b)

\displaystyle\|a^{-3}{\text{{vol}}}_{g}-{\text{{vol}}}_{Bang}\|_{C^{14}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.2c)

\displaystyle\|a^{3}\partial_{t}\phi-(\Psi_{Bang}+C)\|_{C^{14}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.2d)

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\nabla}\phi(t,\cdot)-\hat{\nabla}\phi(t_{0},\cdot)+\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot\hat{\nabla}\Psi_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{14}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.2e)

\displaystyle\|a^{3}k-K_{Bang}\|_{C_{\gamma}^{14}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.2f)

\displaystyle\left\|g\odot\exp\left[-2\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot K_{Bang}\right]-M_{Bang}\right\|_{C^{12}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Furthermore, one has

(8.3a)

8\pi(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{2}+\lvert K_{Bang}\rvert^{2}=12\pi C^{2},\quad{(K_{Bang})^{a}}_{a}=-\sqrt{12\pi}C

and, denoting
{\left(K_{Bang}^{\parallel}\right)^{i}}_{j}={(K_{Bang})^{i}}_{j}+\sqrt{\frac{4\pi}{3}}C\mathbb{I}^{i}_{j}
,

(8.3b)

\|{\text{{vol}}}_{\gamma}-{\text{{vol}}}_{Bang}\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|M_{Bang}-\gamma\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|K_{Bang}^{\parallel}\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}+\|\Psi\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim\varepsilon\,.

Additionally, there exists
f_{\mathrm{Bang}}\in C^{10}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)\,
such that, for any
t\in(t,t_{0}]
, one has

(8.4a)

\|f-f_{\mathrm{Bang}}\|_{C^{10}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

where the footprint state
f_{\mathrm{Bang}}
can be controlled as follows.

(8.4b)

\|f_{\mathrm{Bang}}-f_{FLRW}\|_{C^{10}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}

The momentum support of
f_{\mathrm{Bang}}
is bounded and, in the massless case, bounded away from the origin with respect to
\underline{\gamma}|_{vert}
. The solution norm bound (
8.1a
) also implies the following Vlasov matter bounds:

(8.4c)

\displaystyle\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{H^{18}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.4d)

\displaystyle\left\|T_{00}^{Vl}[\overline{g},{f}]-T_{00}^{Vl}[\overline{g}_{FLRW},f_{FLRW}]\right\|_{C^{16}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.4e)

\displaystyle\left\|T_{0(\cdot)}^{Vl}[\overline{g},{f}]-T_{0(\cdot)}^{Vl}[\overline{g}_{FLRW},{f}_{FLRW}]\right\|_{C^{16}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-3-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.4f)

\displaystyle\left\|T_{(\cdot)(\cdot)}^{Vl}[\overline{g},{f}]-T_{(\cdot)(\cdot)}^{Vl}[\overline{g}_{FLRW},{f}_{FLRW}]\right\|_{C^{16}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a(t)^{-4-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

The FLRW solution exhibits stable mean, scalar, Ricci and Kretschmann curvature scalar blow-up toward the Big Bang in the following sense:

(8.5a)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{6}\lvert k\rvert_{g}^{2}-\left\lvert K_{Bang}\right\rvert^{2}\right\|_{C^{0}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.5b)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{6}R[\overline{g}]-8\pi(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{2}\right\|_{C^{0}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.5c)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{12}\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]_{\alpha\beta}\text{{Ric}}[\overline{g}]^{\alpha\beta}-(8\pi)^{2}(\Psi_{Bang}+C)^{4}\right\|_{C^{0}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

(8.5d)

\displaystyle\left\|a^{12}\mathcal{K}-\frac{5}{2}\cdot(8\pi)^{2}(\Psi+C)^{2}\right\|_{C^{0}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Finally,
\left(\overline{M},\overline{g}\right)
is causally disconnected and geodesically incomplete toward the past in the same sense as in
[
FU24
, Theorem 8.2]
.

Remark 8.2

(On AVTD behaviour)
.

(
8.2
) demonstrates that the evolution of metric and scalar field are asymptotically velocity term dominated (AVTD) in the sense that
(g,k,n,\partial_{0}\phi)
exhibit asymptotic behaviour which, agrees at first order with the solution to the truncated Einstein Vlasov scalar-field system in CMC gauge in which we drop all spatial derivatives from (
2.20a
), (
2.20c
), (
2.20f
) and (
2.20p
). Note that, when considering the evolution of
{k^{i}}_{j}
(or, equivalently, its tracefree part
{\hat{k}^{i}}_{j}
) as well as the lapse equation, Vlasov matter terms are not truncated, leading to lower order terms in the VTD solution – as opposed to the pure scalar-field case, where the VTD solution is simply described by
a^{-3}K_{Bang}
. Similarly truncating the rescaled Vlasov equation (
2.19a
) simply leads to the equation
\partial_{t}f_{VTD}=0
, if one views the full Sasaki derivative
\underline{\nabla}_{i}
as the horizontal term to be truncated as the form of the isotropic solution suggests. Thus, the Vlasov distribution on the co-mass shell is also AVTD, but one loses control of the derived energy-momentum quantities since the leading order term is influenced by the level of inhomogeneity encoded in even the formal VTD solution via the asymptotics of the spatial metric. Thus, these asymptotics are in line with the critical behaviour of radiation fluids discussed in Section
1.3.2
, since the corresponding macroscopic quantities degenerate towards the Big Bang, but at controllable rates.

Proof of Theorem
8.1
.

Firstly, we can assume without loss of generality that the initial data is CMC by Remark
3.7
. If
\varepsilon
is chosen sufficiently small, Lemma
3.9
implies that there is a past maximal solution
(\overline{M},\overline{g},\phi,\overline{f})
to the ESFV-system with
\overline{M}\simeq(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]\times M
for
\mathfrak{t}\in[0,t_{0}]
. Furthermore, we can assume sufficient regularity for the initial data such that all norms and energies are continuous in time (see Remark
3.7
)
5
5

5

In particular, for any
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we again note that the support assumptions on
\mathring{f}
ensure that
\mathring{f}\in H^{l}_{1,{\underline{\gamma}}_{0},m}(T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}})
is equivalent to
\mathring{f}\in H^{l}_{\mu,{\underline{\gamma}}_{0},m}(T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}})
for any
\mu\geq 0
.
.

Thus, for some suitable
K_{0}>0
, the bootstrap assumptions hold on
(t_{Boot},t_{0}]\times M
for
t_{Boot}\in[\mathfrak{t},t_{0})
(see Assumption
3.11
). If
\varepsilon>0
is, again, chosen to be small enough, the assumption (
3.13a
) is improved by Corollary
7.10
, and (
3.13b
) is improved by (
4.16
). Thus, (
8.1a
) and (
8.1b
) hold on
(\mathfrak{t},t_{0}]
, and consequently (
8.2
) as in the proof of
[
FU24
, Theorem 8.2]
. In particular, if
\mathfrak{t}>0
were to hold, none of the blow-up criteria in Lemma
3.9
would be satisfied approaching
\mathfrak{t}
:

(1)

By (
7.12
), the eigenvalues of
(G^{-1})^{ij}
differ from those of
(\gamma^{-1})^{ij}
by at most
K\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
for some
K>0
, and are thus bounded from above by
1+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}
up to multiplicative constant. Hence, the eigenvalues of
G_{ij}
are bounded away from
0
.

(2)

(
8.2a
) prevents (2).

(3)

(
8.2c
) prevents (3).

(4)

(
3.11a
) and (
3.11b
) are directly prevented by (
8.1a
). Regarding (
3.11c
), we need to verify after scaling transformation that

(8.6)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}^{2\cdot 3}\cdot\left(\frac{\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}}{v^{0}_{\gamma}}\right)^{2l}\left(\lvert\underline{\nabla}_{vert}^{\leq 1}f\rvert_{\underline{\gamma}_{0}}^{2}\right)\mu_{\gamma}^{-1}dv<\infty

holds, where
l\geq 18
is a sufficiently large natural number. For the massive case, the factor containing
l
is simply
1
. Thus, expanding
f
into
f_{FLRW}+(f-f_{FLRW})
, and using (
8.2f
), (
8.1a
) and the momentum support bound (
4.6a
), (
8.6
) is bounded by

\lesssim\|f_{FLRW}\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{3,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)}+\left(1+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{C^{0}_{G}(M)}\right)\cdot(\mathcal{P}^{0})^{4}\cdot\mathcal{H}^{2}\lesssim 1+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{2}}a^{-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

In the massless case, (
4.8
) implies

\left(\frac{\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}}{v^{0}_{\gamma}}\right)^{2l}=(1+\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{-2})^{l}\lesssim 1\,,

and thus the same upper bound holds after updating constants.

Hence, one must have
\mathfrak{t}=0
. (
8.3a
)-(
8.3b
) follow directly from (
8.2
), the rescaled constraint equations (
2.20h
) and (
2.20i
) and the initial data assumption, as in the proof of
[
FU24
, (8.4)-(8.6)]
.

Regarding (
8.4a
), we use (
8.1a
) and (
8.1b
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{d}{dt}f(t,x,v_{i})\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\|N\|_{C^{1}_{G}(M_{t})}+\|f-f_{FLRW}\|_{\dot{C}^{1}_{1,\underline{G}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M_{t})}+\|G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\|_{C^{1}(M_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon a^{-1-c{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Further, recall that
\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}=\sqrt{1+\lvert v\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}}
remains bounded on the momentum support of
f
, see (
4.6b
). Thus, one has

\left\lvert\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}\frac{d}{dt}f(t,x,v)\right\rvert\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,.

By integrating in time, this implies for any
s,t\in(0,t_{0})
that

\sup_{{x,v}\in T^{\ast}M}\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}\left\lvert f(t,x,v)-f(s,x,v)\right\rvert\lesssim\sqrt{\varepsilon}\left\lvert a(t)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}-a(s)^{2-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\right\rvert

holds and thus that
f
converges to a continuous function
f_{Bang}
in
C^{0}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)
uniformly when approaching the Big Bang singularity. The bound (
8.4b
) in
C^{0}_{1,\underline{\gamma}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)
then follows directly from above for
t=t_{0}
and
s\downarrow 0
. For higher orders, the argument is analogous, instead considering

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}^{L-K}\,\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L,K}f\right)=\langle v\rangle_{\gamma}^{L-K}\,\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L,K}\left(\bm{X}f\right)\,,

where
\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{L,K}
denotes as combination of
L-K
vertical and
K
horizontal covariant derivatives with respect to
(T^{\ast}M,\underline{\gamma})
. More precisely, it goes through as long as (
8.1b
) can be used to control derivatives of one order higher, i.e., the argument extends to
C^{10}_{1,{\underline{\gamma}}_{0}}(T^{\ast}M)
. In particular, note that
\partial_{t}
and covariant derivatives with respect to
\hat{\underline{\nabla}}
commute, and commutation errors between Sasaki metrics with respect to
\gamma
and
G
can be controlled sufficiently by (
8.1a
). The momentum support bounds for
f_{Bang}
are obtained by considering the limit of the support bounds with respect to
\gamma
in Lemma
4.6
.

The remaining Vlasov matter bounds follow directly from (
8.1a
). Everything else is proven identically to
[
FU24
, Theorem 8.2]
and
[
RS18b
, Theorem 15.1]
– we note the convergence rates in (
8.5
) are weaker than in these results since the presence of Vlasov matter leads to a weaker convergence rate for the lapse (see (
8.2a
)).
∎

It was not necessary in the analysis above to assume that the momentum support of the initial perturbation was close to that of the FLRW solution, as long as their joint support was uniformly bounded and, in the massless case, bounded away from zero. However, if one additionally requires this, this property is preserved throughout the evolution.

Corollary 8.3
.

Consider the setting of Theorem
8.1
and let
\mathrm{dist}_{\underline{\gamma}}
denote the distance function on
T^{\ast}M
induced by the Sasaki metric
\underline{\gamma}
. If one additionally assumes

(8.7)

\mathrm{dist}_{\underline{\gamma}}(\mathrm{supp}\mathring{f},\mathrm{supp}f_{FLRW})\leq\delta

for some
\delta>0
, then the following holds for the limiting Vlasov distribution
f_{Bang}
on the Big Bang hypersurface.

(8.8)

\mathrm{dist}_{\underline{\gamma}}(\mathrm{supp}f_{\mathrm{Bang}},\mathrm{supp}f_{FLRW})\lesssim\delta+\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}\,.

Proof.

Let
(\hat{X}_{s}(t;x,v),\hat{V}_{t}(s;x,v))
denote the characteristics of the FLRW solution, emanating from
(x,v)\in T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}}
, i.e., the solution to the following ODE.

(8.9a)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{\hat{X}_{s}}^{i}=

\displaystyle\,a^{-1}\,(\gamma^{-1})^{ij}\,\frac{\hat{V}_{i}}{\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert\hat{V}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hat{X}_{s}(s;x,v)=

\displaystyle\,x

(8.9b)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(\hat{V}_{s})_{i}=

\displaystyle\,a^{-1}\,(\gamma^{-1})^{jk}\,\hat{\Gamma}^{l}_{ik}\,\frac{\hat{V}_{j}\hat{V}_{l}}{\sqrt{m^{2}a^{2}+\lvert\hat{V}\rvert_{\gamma}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\hat{V}_{s}(s;x,v)=

\displaystyle\,v

Our first goal is to prove that these characteristics are close to the characteristics
(X,V)
of f given by (
4.5
), i.e., that

(8.10)

\lvert X_{t_{0}}-\hat{X}_{t_{0}}\rvert_{\gamma}+\lvert V_{t_{0}}-\hat{V}_{t_{0}}\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}\,.

holds on
(t,x,v)\in(0,t_{0}]\times T^{\ast}M
. As in the proof of Lemma
4.9
, the characteristic equations for
X
and
\hat{X}
ensure that, for a suitable finite partition
\{(t_{j},t_{j}-1]\}_{j=1,\dots,J}
of
(0,t_{0}]
, we can choose a finite covering of
M
such that any characteristic remains contained in a coordinate neighbourhood for each step of the partition. We can also choose this neighbourhood such that Christoffel symbols
\Gamma
and
\hat{\Gamma}
are Lipschitz continuous. it is sufficient to prove

(8.11)

\lvert(X_{t_{j-1}}-\hat{X}_{t_{j-1}})(t,x,v)\rvert_{\gamma}+\lvert(V_{t_{j-1}}-\hat{V}_{t_{j-1}})(t,x,v)\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}

for any
t\in(t_{j},t_{j-1}]
and
(x,v)
in within a coordinate neighbourhood of
(t_{j},t_{j-1}]\times T^{\ast}M
. We will drop the subscript
t_{j-1}
for the proof (
8.11
) for notational convenience.

Using (
8.1a
) as well as Lemma
4.6
, (
8.9
) and (
4.5
) imply the following bound on such a neighbourhood:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{d}{dt}\left(V-\hat{V}\right)\right\rvert\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\,\left(\lvert N\rvert+\lvert G^{-1}-\gamma^{-1}\rvert_{\gamma}+\lvert\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\rvert_{\gamma}+\frac{\lvert V\rvert_{\gamma}+\lvert\hat{V}\rvert_{\gamma}}{V^{0}}\,\lvert V-\hat{V}\rvert_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}(\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}+\lvert V-\hat{V}\rvert_{\gamma})

Since one has
(V-\hat{V})(t_{j-1},\cdot,\cdot)=0
, the Gronwall lemma implies

\lvert(V-\hat{V})_{t_{0}}(t,x,v)\rvert\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}

using (
2.6
). Consequently, one has

\displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{d}{dt}\left(X-\hat{X}\right)\right\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}\left(\lvert N\rvert+\lvert\Gamma-\hat{\Gamma}\rvert_{\gamma}+\lvert V-\hat{V}\rvert_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}a^{-1-c\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

and we obtain (
8.11
) after integrating. By patching coordinate neighbourhoods and iterating over
j=1,\dots,J
, this implies (
8.10
).

Now, note that solutions to (
8.9
) depend on their initial data Lipschitz continuously with respect to
\gamma
due to our coordinate choice. Thus, if one has

\mathrm{dist}_{\underline{\gamma}}((y,w),\mathrm{supp}f_{FLRW}(t_{0},\cdot,\cdot))\leq\delta

for some
(y,w)\in T^{\ast}M_{t_{0}}
, then one has

(8.12)

\mathrm{dist}_{\underline{\gamma}}((\hat{X},\hat{V})_{t_{0}}(0;y,w),\mathrm{supp}f_{FLRW}(0,\cdot,\cdot))\lesssim\delta

since
f_{FLRW}
is time-independent. Finally, for any
(x,v)\in\mathrm{supp}f_{\mathrm{Bang}}
, one has
(X_{0},V_{0})(t_{0};x,v)\in\mathrm{supp}\mathring{f}
. By assumption, we can apply (
8.12
) to the characteristic flow of this point with respect to the
(\hat{X},\hat{V})
-characteristics and the distance of the end point of this characterstic to
(x,v)
is bounded up to a constant by
\varepsilon^{\frac{7}{4}}
due to (
8.10
). This implies (
8.8
).
∎

Additionally, the following analogue of (
8.2f
) holds and admits a limiting renormalised metric on the Big Bang hypersurface in the style used in
[
GPR23
]
.

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
(\overline{M},\overline{g},\phi,f)
be as in Theorem
8.1
and consider the renormalised metrics

\mathcal{G}_{t}(X,Y)=a(t)^{-2}\,\left(g_{t}(\mathcal{W}_{t}(X),\mathcal{W}_{t}(Y))\right)\,,

where

\mathcal{W}_{t,x}(w)=\exp\left[-\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot(\Sigma^{\sharp})_{t,x}\right]\odot w\,.

Then, there exists a Riemannian metric
\mathcal{G}_{Bang}
on
M
such that the following holds.

(8.13)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}_{Bang}-\gamma\|_{C^{15}_{\gamma}(M)}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon

(8.14)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{G}_{Bang}\|_{C^{14}_{\gamma}(M_{t})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,\varepsilon\,a(t)^{2-c\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Proof.

The evolution equation (
2.20a
) implies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}_{ij}=

\displaystyle\,-2\,a^{-3}\,(N+1)\,\Sigma_{rs}\,{\mathcal{W}^{r}}_{i}\,{\mathcal{W}^{s}}_{j}+2\,N\,\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\,\mathcal{G}_{ij}

\displaystyle\,+2\,a^{-2}\,G_{rs}\,(\Sigma^{\sharp})^{l}_{\ i}{\mathcal{W}^{r}}_{l}\,{\mathcal{W}^{s}}_{j}

\displaystyle\,-2\,\int_{(\cdot)}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot G_{rs}\,(\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp})^{l}_{\ i}\,{\mathcal{W}^{r}}_{l}\,{\mathcal{W}^{s}}_{j}\,.

Using that
\Sigma^{\sharp}
and, consequently,
\mathcal{W}
are self-adjoint with respect to
G
, the second line cancels with terms in the first, leading to

\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}_{ij}=2\,N\,\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\,\mathcal{G}_{ij}-2\,a^{-3}\,N\,\Sigma_{rs}\,{\mathcal{W}^{r}}_{i}\,{\mathcal{W}^{s}}_{j}-2\,G_{rs}\,\int_{(\cdot)}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\,(\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp})^{l}_{\ i}\,{\mathcal{W}^{r}}_{l}\,{\mathcal{W}^{s}}_{j}\,.

Note that Theorem
8.1
implies the following bounds for some
c>0
, using Lemma
2.2
and (
2.20c
).

\displaystyle\lvert\mathcal{W}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,a^{-c\,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\lvert\partial_{t}\Sigma^{\sharp}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim

\displaystyle\,{\varepsilon}\,a^{-1-c\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}

Additionally controlling the lapse with (
8.2a
), one has

\lvert\partial_{t}\mathcal{G}\rvert_{\gamma}\lesssim\varepsilon\,a^{-1-c\,\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{8}}}(\lvert\mathcal{G}\rvert_{\gamma}+1)\,.

The existence of
\mathcal{G}_{Bang}
as well as the bounds (
8.13
)-(
8.14
) at order
0
now follow as in the proof of Theorem
8.1
. At higher orders, the respective estimates are obtained similarly.
∎

Remark 8.5

(Interpreting the asymptotics on the mass shell)
.

One might hope to obtain similar asymptotics for the Vlasov distribution on the mass shell. In particular, when interpreting the Vlasov distribution as a kinetic description of particle trajectories, the velocity of a particle is more naturally understood as a section of the mass shell, while the co-mass shell is the more natural choice from the Hamiltonian perspective. However, even on the level of the VTD solution, understanding the picture on the mass shell becomes difficult: Viewing the Vlasov equation (
3.12
) on the mass shell, it is convenient to view this equation in CMCTC gauge in expansion normalised momentum variables
w^{i}=a(t)^{2}q^{i}
, the characteristics of which are clearly preserved in the FLRW case, and to study

f^{\sharp}_{\mathrm{resc}}(t,x,w):=f^{\sharp}(t,x,a(t)^{-2}w).

Truncating the resulting evolution equation in terms of these variables and denoting the lapse and shear of the VTD solution by
n_{VTD}
and
k_{VTD}^{\parallel}
, one obtains the following:

\partial_{t}f^{\sharp}_{VTD}=-2(n_{VTD}-1)\frac{\dot{a}}{a}w^{j}-2n_{VTD}{(k_{VTD}^{\parallel})^{j}}_{i}w^{i}\partial_{w^{j}}f_{VTD}

For initial data
\mathring{f}^{\sharp}
, this transport equation with initial data at
M_{t_{0}}
is solved by
f^{\sharp}_{VTD}(t,w)=\mathring{f}(x,W_{t_{0}}(t,x,w))
, where the characteristic
W_{t_{0}}
obeys

(8.15)

\dot{W}^{j}=-2(n_{VTD}-1)\frac{\dot{a}}{a}W^{j}+2{(k_{VTD}^{\parallel})^{j}}_{i}W^{i}\,.

as well as
W_{t_{0}}(t,x,w)=q
. Since
n_{VTD}
converges to
1
, the leading term in (
8.15
) is given by the leading term of the shear, i.e., one roughly has

(8.16)

W_{t_{0}}(t,x,w)=\exp\left[2\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot(K_{VTD}^{\parallel})_{x}\right]\odot w+\langle\text{lower order terms}\rangle\,.

When performing the analysis above for
f^{\sharp}
, one must thus renormalise the particle velocities as follows to account for the VTD behaviour

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}_{\text{asymp}}(t,x,w)=

\displaystyle\,f^{\sharp}_{\mathrm{resc}}\left(t,x,\exp\left[-2\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot(K_{Bang}^{\parallel})_{x}\right]\odot w\right)\,.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,f^{\sharp}\left(t,x,\exp\left[-2\int_{t}^{t_{0}}a(s)^{-3}\,ds\cdot(K_{Bang})_{x}\right]\odot q\right)\,.

Indeed,
f^{\sharp}_{\text{asymp}}
converges toward the Big Bang in a similar manner to
f
, as can be proven by a similar argument to that on the co-mass shell. However, these results can not be directly translated into one another on the Big Bang hypersurface itself since the spatial metric degenerates.

To interpret this asymptotic profile, we analyze
(K_{Bang}^{\parallel})_{x}
more closely for an arbitrary point
x\in M
: First, we note that
\Sigma^{\sharp}
is self-adjoint with respect to
\mathcal{G}
on
M_{t}
for any
t\in(0,t_{0}]
and, consequently,
K_{Bang}^{\parallel}
is self-adjoint with respect to
\mathcal{G}_{Bang}
by taking the limit. In particular,
K_{Bang}^{\parallel}
is diagonalisable. Let
E_{x,+},E_{x,-}
and
E_{x,0}
denote the respective span of eigenspaces associated to positive, zero and negative eigenvalues of
(K_{Bang}^{\parallel})_{x}
. Note that, if
q\in T^{\ast}_{x}M
is not orthogonal to
E_{x,+}
,
W_{t_{0}}(t,x,w(q))
diverges as
t\downarrow 0
, and thus leaves the support of
f
if
t
is sufficiently close to
0
. Thus, approaching the Big Bang, the majority of particles have momenta that are orthogonal to
E_{x,+}
. Elements of
E_{x,0}
are left unchanged by the evolution, while momenta in
E_{x,-}
approach the origin as
t\downarrow 0
. Thus, Vlasov particle velocities concentrate in the following manner, depending on the signs of eigenvalues of
(K_{Bang}^{\parallel})_{x}
:

(1)

(+,+,-)
or
(+,-,-)
: Particle velocities are attracted by
E_{x,-}
.

(2)

(+,0,-)
: Particle velocities concentrate close to the Minkowski sum
E_{x,-}+\left[E_{x,0}\cap\mathrm{supp}\mathring{f}(x,\cdot)\right]
.

(3)

(0,0,0)
: This implies
f^{\sharp}_{\text{asymp}}(t,x,\cdot)=\mathring{f}(x,\cdot)
for any
t\in(0,t_{0})
, and particles are asymptotically distributed close to the FLRW distribution function.

If the momentum support of
\mathring{f}
is bounded away from the origin, the same interpretations hold, where
E_{x,-}
can be replaced with
\lim_{r\to\infty}E_{x,-}\cap\{w\in T_{x}M|\lvert q|_{\gamma}=r\}
. Recall that we assume this property for
f_{FLRW}
and
\mathring{f}
in the massless case.
